\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{U\otimes Y_{n}}}\equiv X^{\prime}\otimes Y_{n}\;\mathsf{sort}

is derivable. But that follows from
Sub^{1}_{s,s}(h-1)
with
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
replaced by
\partial\textbf{Y}\vdash Y_{n}\;\mathsf{sort}
, noting that

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{Y}\vdash Y_{n}\;\mathsf{sort})=\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h-1,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U[\textbf{f}]\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{Y})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U[\textbf{f}]\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{Y}\vdash Y_{n}\;\mathsf{sort})=\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h-1.

Proof of
Mor^{2}_{+}(h)

Suppose given a context
\textbf{X}=(x_{1}:X_{1},...,x_{m}:X_{m})
in
\mathbb{A}
, and a morphism

\textbf{g}=(g_{1},...,g_{N}):\textbf{Y}=(y_{1}:Y_{1},...,y_{n}:Y_{n})\longrightarrow\textbf{B}=(b_{1}:B_{1},...,b_{N}:B_{N})

and a derivable judgment
\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
in
\mathbb{B}
. Assume that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h-1
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V[\textbf{g}]\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h
. Consider contexts
\textbf{B}^{\prime}=(\textbf{B},b^{\prime}:V)
and
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y^{\prime}:V[\textbf{g}])
, and write
\textbf{g}^{\prime}
for the tuple
(g_{1},...,g_{N},y^{\prime})
. We must verify that

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}^{\prime}

is a context morphism.

Firstly, note that as
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}^{\prime})
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}^{\prime})\leq h
, we obtain from
Cont(h)
that
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
and
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}^{\prime}
are contexts.

Moreover, by
Mor^{2}(h-1)
we have a context morphism
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}
, thus also a morphism

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}.

If
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})=0
, then the tuple
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime}
, which is empty, is the identity of the empty context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
. Otherwise, we have
\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{X})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})
, so we can use
Mor^{2}_{+}(h-1)
to derive
\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime}:\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\rightarrow\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}^{\prime}
, hence

\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}^{\prime}.

From (
*
) and (
**
) we obtain a morphism

\partial(\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime}):\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\partial(\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}^{\prime}).

To conclude, using a double bar to indicate substitution along
\partial(\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime})
, let us verify that the judgment

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash x_{m}y^{\prime}:\overline{\overline{X_{m}\otimes V}}

is derivable. It suffices to prove that

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\overline{\overline{X_{m}\otimes V}}\equiv X_{m}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}

is derivable. But that follows from
Sub^{2}_{s,s}(h-1)
with
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
replaced by
\partial\textbf{X}\vdash Y_{m}\;\mathsf{sort}
, noting that

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{X}\vdash X_{m}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})=\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h-1,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V[\textbf{g}])<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash V[\textbf{g}])\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{X}\vdash X_{m}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})=\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})\leq h-1.

Proof of
Sub^{1}_{t,s}(h)

Suppose given a context morphism

\textbf{f}=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{M}):\textbf{X}=(x_{1}:X_{1},\ldots,x_{m}:X_{m})\longrightarrow\textbf{A}=(a_{1}:A_{1},\ldots,a_{M}:A_{M})

in
\mathbb{A}
, a context
\textbf{Y}=(y_{1}:Y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}:Y_{n})
in
\mathbb{B}
, and derivable judgments
\textbf{A}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term}
,
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
are at most
h
.

Let
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y^{\prime}:V)
. Since
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}^{\prime})=\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h
, by
Cont(h)
we have that
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
is a context.

Using notation analogous to that of the previous lemma, we will verify by induction on
P\in\{0,\ldots,h\}
that

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\overline{u\otimes y^{\prime}}\equiv\underline{u}\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}

is derivable whenever
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})=P
.

Assume that the claim holds for all
P^{\prime}<P=\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
.

If
u
is a variable
a_{I}
, then

\overline{u\otimes y^{\prime}}=\overline{a_{I}y^{\prime}}=f_{I}\otimes y^{\prime}=\underline{u}\otimes y^{\prime}.

If it is not a variable, let
u=s(s_{1},\ldots,s_{p})
and
V=T(t_{1},\ldots,t_{q})
. Then

\underline{u}\otimes y^{\prime}=s(\underline{s_{1}},\ldots,\underline{s_{p}})\otimes T(t_{1},\ldots,t_{q})=sT((\underline{s_{1}},\ldots,\underline{s_{p}})\otimes(t_{1},\ldots,t_{q},y^{\prime})).

Now, for
1\leq i\leq p
and
1\leq j\leq q
, note that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash s_{i}\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash t_{j}\;\mathsf{term})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h
, so by
Sub^{1}_{t,t}(h-1)
we can derive
\overline{s_{i}\otimes t_{j}}\equiv\underline{s_{i}}\otimes t_{j}
in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
, hence in
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
.

Moreover, for
1\leq i\leq p
we have
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash s_{i}\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
, so by the induction hypothesis we can derive
\overline{s_{i}\otimes y^{\prime}}\equiv\underline{s_{i}}\otimes y^{\prime}
in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
.

It follows that

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\overline{sT((s_{1},\ldots,s_{p})\otimes(t_{1},\ldots,t_{q},y^{\prime}))}\equiv sT((\underline{s_{1}},\ldots,\underline{s_{p}})\otimes(t_{1},\ldots,t_{q},y^{\prime}))\;\mathsf{term}

is derivable. But this judgment is precisely
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\overline{u\otimes y^{\prime}}\equiv\underline{u}\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}
.

Proof of
Sub^{2}_{s,t}(h)

Suppose given a context
\textbf{X}=(x_{1}:X_{1},...,x_{m}:X_{m})
in
\mathbb{A}
, a context morphism

\textbf{g}:(g_{1},...,g_{N}):\textbf{Y}=(y_{1}:Y_{1},...,y_{n}:Y_{n})\longrightarrow\textbf{B}=(b_{1}:B_{1},...,b_{N}:B_{N})

in
\mathbb{B}
, and derivable judgments
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
,
\textbf{B}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term}
such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term})
are at most
h
.

Let
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x^{\prime}:U)
. Since
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}^{\prime})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})=\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h
, by
Cont(h)
we have that
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
is a context.

In notation analogous to that of the previous lemma, we will verify by induction on
P\in\{0,...,h\}
that

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{x^{\prime}\otimes v}}\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes\underline{v}\;\mathsf{term}

is derivable if
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term})=P
.

Assume that the claim holds for all
P^{\prime}<P=\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term})
.

If
v
is a variable
b_{J}
, then

\overline{\overline{x^{\prime}\otimes v}}=\overline{\overline{x^{\prime}b_{J}}}=x^{\prime}\otimes g_{J}=x^{\prime}\otimes\underline{v}.

If it is not a variable, let
U=S(s_{1},...,s_{p})
and
v=t(t_{1},...,t_{q})
. Then

x^{\prime}\otimes\underline{v}=S(s_{1},...,s_{p})\otimes t(\underline{t_{1}},...,\underline{t_{q}})=St((s_{1},...,s_{p},x^{\prime})\otimes(\underline{t_{1}},...,\underline{t_{q}})).

Now, for
1\leq i\leq p
and
1\leq j\leq q
, note that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash s_{i}\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash t_{j}\;\mathsf{term})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term})\leq h
, so by
Sub^{2}_{t,t}(h-1)
we can derive
\overline{\overline{s_{i}\otimes t_{j}}}\equiv s_{i}\otimes\underline{t_{j}}
in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
, hence in
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
.

Moreover, for
1\leq j\leq q
we have
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash t_{j}\;\mathsf{term})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term})\leq h
, so by the induction hypothesis we can derive
\overline{\overline{x^{\prime}\otimes t_{j}}}\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes\underline{t_{j}}
in context
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
.

It follows that

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{St((s_{1},...,s_{p},x^{\prime})\otimes(t_{1},...,t_{q}))}}\equiv St((s_{1},...,s_{p},x^{\prime})\otimes(\underline{t_{1}},...,\underline{t_{q}}))\;\mathsf{term}

is derivable. But this judgment is precisely
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{x^{\prime}\otimes v}}\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes\underline{v}\;\mathsf{term}
.

Proof of
Sub^{1}_{s,t}(h)

Suppose given a context morphism

\textbf{f}=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{M}):\textbf{X}=(x_{1}:X_{1},\ldots,x_{m}:X_{m})\longrightarrow\textbf{A}=(a_{1}:A_{1},\ldots,a_{M}:A_{M})

in
\mathbb{A}
, a context
\textbf{Y}=(y_{1}:Y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}:Y_{n})
in
\mathbb{B}
, and derivable judgments
\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
,
\textbf{Y}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term}
such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term})
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U[\textbf{f}]\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term})
are at most
h
.

Let
\textbf{A}^{\prime}=(\textbf{A},a^{\prime}:U)
and
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x^{\prime}:\underline{U})
where
\underline{U}=U[\textbf{f}]
. Since
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}^{\prime})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h
, by
Cont(h)
we have that
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
is a context.

We will verify by induction on
P\in\{0,\ldots,h\}
that

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{a^{\prime}\otimes v}}\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes v\;\mathsf{term}

is derivable whenever
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term})=P
.

In fact, we will start by verifying, without using the induction hypothesis, that
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{a^{\prime}\otimes v}}\;\mathsf{term}
is derivable; the induction hypothesis will then be used to derive the equality with
x^{\prime}\otimes v
in context
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
, which in particular will imply that
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes v\;\mathsf{term}
is derivable.

For the first part, note that, as

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash\underline{U}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h,

by
Mor^{1}_{+}(h)
we can derive a morphism

\textbf{f}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\textbf{A}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}.

Moreover, as
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term})\leq h
, by
h
-derivability we can tensor
\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term}
to derive

\textbf{A}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash a^{\prime}\otimes v\;\mathsf{term}.

By substitution of (
**
) along (
*
) we derive
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{a^{\prime}\otimes v}}\;\mathsf{term}
.

Now, let us turn to the inductive argument. Assume that the claim holds for all
P^{\prime}<P=\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term})
.

If
v
is a variable
y_{J}
, then

\overline{\overline{a^{\prime}\otimes v}}=\overline{\overline{a^{\prime}y_{J}}}=x^{\prime}y_{J}=\underline{a^{\prime}}\otimes v.

If it is not a variable, let
U=S(s_{1},...,s_{p})
and
v=t(t_{1},...,t_{q})
. Then
\underline{U}=S(\underline{s_{1}},...,\underline{s_{p}})
, so

x^{\prime}\otimes v=S(\underline{s_{1}},...,\underline{s_{p}})\otimes t(t_{1},...,t_{q})=St((\underline{s_{1}},...,\underline{s_{p}},x^{\prime})\otimes(t_{1},...,t_{q})).

For
1\leq i\leq p
and
1\leq j\leq q
, note that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash s_{i}\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash t_{j}\;\mathsf{term})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term})\leq h
, so by
Sub^{1}_{t,t}(h-1)
we can derive
\overline{\overline{s_{i}\otimes t_{j}}}\equiv\underline{s_{i}}\otimes t_{j}
in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
, hence in
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
.

Also, for
1\leq j\leq q
we have
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash t_{j}\;\mathsf{term})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term})
, so by the induction hypothesis we can derive
\overline{\overline{a^{\prime}\otimes t_{j}}}\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes t_{j}
in context
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
.

It follows that

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{St((s_{1},...,s_{p},a^{\prime})\otimes(t_{1},...,t_{q}))}}\equiv St((\underline{s_{1}},...,\underline{s_{p}},x^{\prime})\otimes(t_{1},...,t_{q}))\;\mathsf{term}

is derivable. But this judgment is precisely
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{a^{\prime}\otimes v}}\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes v\;\mathsf{term}
.

Proof of
Sub^{2}_{t,s}(h)

Suppose given a context
\textbf{X}=(x_{1}:X_{1},...,x_{m}:X_{m})
in
\mathbb{A}
, a context morphism

\textbf{g}:(g_{1},...,g_{N}):\textbf{Y}=(y_{1}:Y_{1},...,y_{n}:Y_{n})\longrightarrow\textbf{B}=(b_{1}:B_{1},...,b_{N}:B_{N})

in
\mathbb{B}
, and derivable judgments
\textbf{X}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term}
,
\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V[\textbf{g}]\;\mathsf{sort})
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
are at most
h
.

Let
\textbf{B}^{\prime}=(\textbf{B},b^{\prime}:V)
and
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y^{\prime}:\underline{V})
where
\underline{V}=V[\textbf{g}]
. Since
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\leq h
, by
Cont(h)
we have that
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
is a context.

We will verify by induction on
P\in\{0,...,h\}
that

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\overline{\overline{u\otimes b^{\prime}}}\equiv u\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}

is derivable whenever
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})=P
.

As in the previous lemma, we will start by verifying, without using the induction hypothesis, that
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\overline{\overline{u\otimes b^{\prime}}}\;\mathsf{term}
is derivable; the we will use the induction hypothesis to derive the equality with
u\otimes y^{\prime}
in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
, which in particular implies that
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash u\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}
is derivable.

For the first part, note that, as

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash\underline{V})\leq h,

by
Mor^{2}_{+}(h)
we can derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}^{\prime}.

Moreover, as
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h
, by
h
-derivability we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
to derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}^{\prime}\vdash u\otimes b^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}.

By substitution of (
**
) along (
*
) we derive
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\overline{\overline{u\otimes b^{\prime}}}\;\mathsf{term}
.

Now, we turn to the inductive argument. Assume that the claim holds for all
P^{\prime}<P=\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
.

If
u
is a variable
a_{I}
, then

\overline{\overline{u\otimes b^{\prime}}}=\overline{\overline{a_{I}b^{\prime}}}=a_{I}y^{\prime}=u\otimes\underline{b^{\prime}}.

If it is not a variable, let
u=s(s_{1},...,s_{p})
and
V=T(t_{1},...,t_{q})
. Then
\underline{V}=T(\underline{t_{1}},...,\underline{t_{q}})
, so

u\otimes y^{\prime}=s(s_{1},...,s_{p})\otimes T(\underline{t_{1}},...,\underline{t_{q}})=sT((s_{1},...,s_{p})\otimes(\underline{t_{1}},...,\underline{t_{1}},y^{\prime})).

For
1\leq i\leq p
and
1\leq j\leq q
, note that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash s_{i}\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash t_{j}\;\mathsf{term})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h
, so by
Sub^{2}_{t,t}(h)
we can derive
\overline{\overline{s_{i}\otimes t_{j}}}\equiv s_{i}\otimes\underline{t_{j}}
in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
, hence in
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
.

Also, for
1\leq i\leq p
we have
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash s_{i}\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
, so by the induction hypothesis we can derive
\overline{\overline{s_{i}\otimes b^{\prime}}}\equiv s_{i}\otimes y^{\prime}
in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
.

It follows that

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\overline{\overline{sT((s_{1},...,s_{p})\otimes(t_{1},...,t_{q},b^{\prime}))}}\equiv sT((s_{1},...,s_{p})\otimes(\underline{t_{1}},...,\underline{t_{q}},y^{\prime}))\;\mathsf{term}

is derivable. But this judgment is precisely
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\overline{\overline{u\otimes b^{\prime}}}\equiv u\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}
.

Proof of
Sub^{1}_{s,s}(h)

Suppose given a context morphism

\textbf{f}=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{M}):\textbf{X}=(x_{1}:X_{1},\ldots,x_{m}:X_{m})\longrightarrow\textbf{A}=(a_{1}:A_{1},\ldots,a_{M}:A_{M})

in
\mathbb{A}
, a context
\textbf{Y}=(y_{1}:Y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}:Y_{n})
in
\mathbb{B}
, and derivable judgments
\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U[\textbf{f}]\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
are at most
h
.

Define contexts
\textbf{A}^{\prime}=(\textbf{A},a^{\prime}:U)
,
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x^{\prime}:\underline{U})
, and
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y^{\prime}:V)
where
\underline{U}=U[\textbf{f}]
. Using the same notation as in the previous lemma, our goal is to derive

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash\overline{\overline{U\otimes V}}\equiv\underline{U}\otimes V\;\mathsf{sort}.

We will prove by induction on
P\in\{0,\ldots,h\}
that this judgment is derivable whenever
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})=P
. Suppose that the claim holds for all
P^{\prime}<P=\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
.

Let
U=S(s_{1},...,s_{k})
and
V=T(t_{1},...,t_{l})
. Then

\overline{\overline{U\otimes V}}=\overline{\overline{ST(\partial((s_{1},...,s_{k},a^{\prime})\otimes(t_{1},...,t_{l},y^{\prime})))}},

which can be written in matrix form as

ST\begin{pmatrix}\overline{\overline{s_{1}\otimes t_{1}}}&\cdots&\overline{\overline{s_{1}\otimes t_{l}}}&\overline{\overline{s_{1}\otimes y^{\prime}}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\overline{\overline{s_{k}\otimes t_{1}}}&\cdots&\overline{\overline{s_{k}\otimes t_{l}}}&\overline{\overline{s_{k}\otimes y^{\prime}}}\\
\overline{\overline{a^{\prime}\otimes t_{1}}}&\cdots&\overline{\overline{a^{\prime}\otimes t_{l}}}&-\\
\end{pmatrix}.

We can describe each entry as follows:

•

For
i\leq k
and
j\leq l
, the expression
\overline{\overline{s_{i}\otimes t_{j}}}
is obtained from
s_{i}\otimes t_{j}
by substitution along
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}
. But since

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash s_{i}\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash t_{j}\;\mathsf{term})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash t_{j}\;\mathsf{term})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h,

by
Sub^{1}_{t,t}(h-1)
we can derive
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{s_{i}\otimes t_{j}}}\equiv\underline{s_{i}}\otimes t_{j}\;\mathsf{term}
.

•

To describe
\overline{\overline{a^{\prime}\otimes t_{j}}}
, note that since

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash t_{j}\;\mathsf{term})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash\underline{U}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash t_{j}\;\mathsf{term})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h,

we can use
Sub^{1}_{s,t}(h)
to derive

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{a^{\prime}\otimes t_{j}}}\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes t_{j}\;\mathsf{term}.

•

To describe
\overline{\overline{s_{i}\otimes y^{\prime}}}
, since

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash s_{i}\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h,

we can use
Sub^{1}_{t,s}(h)
to derive

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{s_{i}\otimes y^{\prime}}}\equiv\underline{s_{i}}\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}.

We conclude that
\overline{\overline{U\otimes V}}
is provably equal in context
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})
to

ST\begin{pmatrix}\underline{s_{1}}\otimes t_{1}&\cdots&\underline{s_{1}}\otimes t_{l}&\underline{s_{1}}\otimes y^{\prime}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\underline{s_{k}}\otimes t_{1}&\cdots&\underline{s_{k}}\otimes t_{l}&\underline{s_{k}}\otimes y^{\prime}\\
x^{\prime}\otimes t_{1}&\cdots&x^{\prime}\otimes t_{l}&-\\
\end{pmatrix}.

This, in turn, is the matrix form of
\underline{U}\otimes V=ST(\partial((\underline{s_{1}},...,\underline{s_{k}},x^{\prime})\otimes(t_{1},...,t_{l},y^{\prime})))
.

Proof of
Sub^{2}_{s,s}(h)

Suppose given a context
\textbf{X}=(x_{1}:X_{1},...,x_{m}:X_{m})
in
\mathbb{A}
, a context morphism

\textbf{g}:(g_{1},...,g_{N}):\textbf{Y}=(y_{1}:Y_{1},...,y_{n}:Y_{n})\longrightarrow\textbf{B}=(b_{1}:B_{1},...,b_{N}:B_{N})

in
\mathbb{B}
, and derivable judgments
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V[\textbf{g}]\;\mathsf{sort})
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})
are at most
h
.

Define contexts
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x^{\prime}:U)
,
\textbf{B}^{\prime}=(\textbf{B},b^{\prime}:V)
, and
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y^{\prime}:\underline{V})
where
\underline{V}=V[\textbf{g}]
. Using notation analogous to that in the previous lemma, our goal is to derive

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash\overline{\overline{U\otimes V}}\equiv U\otimes\underline{V}\;\mathsf{sort}.

We will prove by induction on
P\in\{0,...,h\}
that this judgment is derivable whenever
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})=P
. Suppose that the claim holds for all
P^{\prime}<P=\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
.

Let
U=S(s_{1},...,s_{k})
and
V=T(t_{1},...,t_{l})
. Then, in matrix form,
\overline{\overline{U\otimes V}}
is

ST\begin{pmatrix}\overline{\overline{s_{1}\otimes t_{1}}}&\cdots&\overline{\overline{s_{1}\otimes t_{l}}}&\overline{\overline{s_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\overline{\overline{s_{k}\otimes t_{1}}}&\cdots&\overline{\overline{s_{k}\otimes t_{l}}}&\overline{\overline{s_{k}\otimes b^{\prime}}}\\
\overline{\overline{x^{\prime}\otimes t_{1}}}&\cdots&\overline{\overline{x^{\prime}\otimes t_{l}}}&-\\
\end{pmatrix}.

We describe each entry as follows:

•

For
i\leq k
and
j\leq l
, the expression
\overline{\overline{s_{i}\otimes t_{j}}}
is obtained from
s_{i}\otimes t_{j}
by substitution along
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}\rightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
. But since

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash s_{i}\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash t_{j}\;\mathsf{term})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash s_{i}\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})\leq h,

by
Sub^{2}_{t,t}(h-1)
we can derive
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{s_{i}\otimes t_{j}}}\equiv s_{i}\otimes\underline{t_{j}}\;\mathsf{term}
.

•

To describe
\overline{\overline{s_{i}\otimes b^{\prime}}}
, note that since

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash s_{i}\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash\underline{V}\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash s_{i}\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h,

we can use
Sub^{2}_{t,s}(h)
to derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\overline{\overline{s_{i}\otimes b^{\prime}}}\equiv s_{i}\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}.

•

To describe
\overline{\overline{x^{\prime}\otimes t_{j}}}
, since

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash t_{j}\;\mathsf{term})\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h,

we can use
Sub^{2}_{s,t}(h)
to derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\overline{\overline{x^{\prime}\otimes t_{j}}}\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes\underline{t_{j}}\;\mathsf{term}.

We conclude that
\overline{\overline{U\otimes V}}
is provably equal in context
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})
to

ST\begin{pmatrix}s_{1}\otimes\underline{t_{1}}&\cdots&s_{1}\otimes\underline{t_{l}}&s_{1}\otimes y^{\prime}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
s_{k}\otimes\underline{t_{1}}&\cdots&s_{k}\otimes\underline{t_{l}}&s_{k}\otimes y^{\prime}\\
x^{\prime}\otimes\underline{t_{1}}&\cdots&x^{\prime}\otimes\underline{t_{l}}&-\\
\end{pmatrix}.

This, in turn, is the matrix form of
U\otimes\underline{V}=ST(\partial((s_{1},...,s_{k},x^{\prime})\otimes(\underline{t_{1}},...,\underline{t_{l}},y^{\prime})))
.

Proof of
Mor^{1}(h)

Write
\mathscr{S}
for the set of all pairs consisting of a context morphism
\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A}
in
\mathbb{A}
and a context
Y
in
\mathbb{B}
such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})=h
. Note that in this case, since
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A})\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})
, by
Cont(h)
we have that
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
and
\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}
are contexts.

We will prove by induction on
L\geq 0
that
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}
is a morphism from
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
to
\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}
whenever
l(\textbf{A})l(\textbf{Y})=L
.

The claim holds for
L=0
as in this case
\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}
is the empty context and
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}
is the empty sequence. Now, let
L\geq 1
and assume that the claim holds for
0
, …,
L-1
. Suppose that
(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A},\textbf{Y})\in\mathscr{S}
satisfies
l(\textbf{A})l(\textbf{Y})=L
.

Since
\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\partial\textbf{A})\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})
and
l(\partial\textbf{A})<l(\textbf{A})
, by the induction hypothesis we have a morphism

\partial\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\partial\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}.

Similarly, as
\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{Y})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})
we have a morphism
\textbf{f}\otimes\partial\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}\otimes\partial\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\textbf{A}\otimes\partial\textbf{Y}
, hence a morphism

\textbf{f}\otimes\partial\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\textbf{A}\otimes\partial\textbf{Y}.

From (
*
) and (
*
*
) we obtain a morphism

\partial(\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}):\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\partial(\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}).

To derive
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}
, it remains to derive the term judgment corresponding to its last entry, i.e.

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash f_{M}\otimes y_{n}:\overline{\overline{A_{M}\otimes Y_{n}}}

where the double bar indicates the substitution operation
[f_{i}\otimes y_{j}\mid a_{i}y_{j}]_{(i,j)<(M,n)}
.

Using a lower bar to indicate the operation
[f_{i}\mid a_{i}]_{i<M}
, we have

\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash f_{M}:\underline{A_{M}})\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{Y}\vdash Y_{n}\;\mathsf{sort})\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})=h,

by
h
-derivability we can derive
(\textbf{X}\vdash f_{M}:\underline{A_{M}})\otimes(\partial\textbf{Y}\vdash Y_{n}\;\mathsf{sort})
, which is

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash f_{M}\otimes y_{n}:(\underline{A_{M}}\otimes Y_{n})[f_{M}\otimes y_{j}]_{j<n}.

Thus it suffices to derive

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y})\vdash\overline{\overline{A_{M}\otimes Y_{n}}}\equiv\underline{A_{M}}\otimes Y_{n}\;\mathsf{sort}

where
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x^{\prime}:\underline{A_{M}})
. But that is obtained from
Sub^{1}_{s,s}(h)
applied to the morphism
\partial\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\partial\textbf{A}
and the judgments
\partial\textbf{A}\vdash A_{M}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\partial\textbf{Y}\vdash Y_{n}\;\mathsf{sort}
, noting that

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{A}\vdash A_{M}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{Y}\vdash Y_{n}\;\mathsf{sort})=\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash\underline{A_{M}}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{Y})\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash f_{M}:\underline{A_{M}})\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{Y})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\partial\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{Y}\vdash Y_{n}\;\mathsf{sort})\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h.

This concludes the proof of the claim for
L
.

Proof of
Mor^{2}(h)

Write
\mathscr{S}
for the set of all pairs consisting of a context
X
in
\mathbb{A}
and a context morphism
\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B}
in
\mathbb{B}
such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})=h
. Note that in this case, since
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B})\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})
, by
Cont(h)
we have that
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
and
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}
are contexts.

We will prove by induction on
L\geq 0
that
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}
is a morphism from
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
to
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}
whenever
l(\textbf{X})l(\textbf{B})=L
.

The claim holds for
L=0
as in this case
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}
is the empty context and
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}
is the empty sequence. Now, let
L\geq 1
and assume that the claim holds for
0
, …,
L-1
. Suppose that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})\in\mathscr{S}
satisfies
l(\textbf{X})l(\textbf{B})=L
.

Since
\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\partial\textbf{B})\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})
and
l(\partial\textbf{B})<l(\textbf{B})
, by the induction hypothesis we have a morphism

\textbf{X}\otimes\partial\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\partial\textbf{B}.

Similarly, as
\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{X})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})
we have a morphism
\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}:\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}
, hence a morphism

\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}.

From (
*
) and (
*
*
) we obtain a morphism

\partial(\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}):\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\partial(\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}).

To derive
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}
, it remains to derive the term judgment corresponding to its last entry, i.e.

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x_{m}\otimes g_{N}:\overline{\overline{X_{m}\otimes B_{N}}}

where the upper double bar indicates the substitution operation
[x_{i}\otimes g_{j}\mid x_{i}b_{j}]_{(i,j)<(m,N)}
.

Using a lower bar to indicate the operation
[g_{i}\mid b_{j}]_{j<N}
, we have

\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{X}\vdash X_{m}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash g_{N}:\underline{B_{N}})\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})=h,

so by
h
-derivability we can derive
(\partial\textbf{X}\vdash X_{m}\;\mathsf{sort})\otimes(\textbf{Y}\vdash g_{N}:\underline{B_{N}})
, which is

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x_{m}\otimes g_{N}:(X_{m}\otimes\underline{B_{N}})[x_{i}\otimes g_{N}]_{i<m}.

Thus it suffices to derive

\partial(\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash\overline{\overline{X_{m}\otimes B_{N}}}\equiv X_{m}\otimes\underline{B_{N}}\;\mathsf{sort}

where
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y^{\prime}:\underline{B_{N}})
. But that is obtained from
Sub^{2}_{s,s}(h)
applied to the morphism
\partial\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\partial\textbf{B}
and the judgments
\partial\textbf{X}\vdash X_{m}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\partial\textbf{B}\vdash B_{N}\;\mathsf{sort}
, noting that

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{X}\vdash X_{m}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{B}\vdash B_{N}\;\mathsf{sort})=\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash\underline{B_{N}}\;\mathsf{sort})\leq\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash g_{N}:\underline{B_{N}})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{X}\vdash X_{m}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\partial\textbf{B})\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})\leq h.

This concludes the proof of the claim for
L
.

4.2
Context equalities and context morphism equalities

We will now consider the following statements:

Conteq^{1}(h)

Suppose given in
\mathbb{A}
a derivable judgment
\textbf{X}\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
, and in
\mathbb{B}
a context
Y
. Let
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x:U)
,
\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}=(\textbf{X},x:U^{\prime})
.

If
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h
, then
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\equiv\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\;\textsf{ctx}
is derivable.

Conteq^{2}(h)

Suppose given in
\mathbb{A}
a context
X
, and in
\mathbb{B}
a derivable judgment
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\equiv V^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
. Let
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y:V)
,
\textbf{Y}^{\prime\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y:V^{\prime})
.

If
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\equiv V^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h
, then
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\equiv\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime\prime}\;\textsf{ctx}
is derivable.

Moreq^{1}(h)

Suppose given in
\mathbb{A}
a derivable morphism equality
\textbf{f}\equiv\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A}
, and in
\mathbb{B}
a context
Y
.

If
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}\equiv\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h
, then
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}\equiv\textbf{g}\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}
is derivable.

Moreq^{1}(h)

Suppose given in
\mathbb{A}
a context
X
, and in
\mathbb{B}
a derivable morphism equality
\textbf{f}\equiv\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B}
.

If
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}\equiv\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})\leq h
, then
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{f}\equiv\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}
is derivable.

Lemma 4.3
.

If
(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{B})
is
h
-derivable, then it satisfies
Conteq^{1}(h)
and
Conteq^{2}(h)
.

Proof.

We will only verify
Conteq^{1}(h)
, the proof of
Conteq^{2}(h)
being analogous.

Suppose given a derivable judgment
\textbf{X}\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
in
\mathbb{A}
and a context
Y
in
\mathbb{B}
such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h
. Let us prove that, considering contexts
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x:U)
and
\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}=(\textbf{X},x:U^{\prime})
, the judgment
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\equiv\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\;\textsf{ctx}
is derivable.

If
Y
is the empty context, the claim is trivial. Otherwise, expressing
Y
as
(\partial\textbf{Y},y:V)
, by
h
-derivability we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\partial\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
to derive

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y})\vdash U\otimes V\equiv U^{\prime}\otimes V\;\mathsf{sort}.

Thus to obtain the desired context equality it remains to derive
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y})\equiv\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y})
. But as
\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{Y})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})
, this follows by using
Conteq^{1}(h-1)
to derive
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\partial\textbf{Y}\equiv\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\partial\textbf{Y}\;\textsf{ctx}
.
∎

Lemma 4.4
.

If
(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{B})
is
h
-derivable, then it satisfies
Moreq^{1}(h)
and
Moreq^{2}(h)
.

Proof.

We will only check
Moreq^{1}(h)
, the proof of
Moreq^{2}(h)
being analogous.

Suppose given a derivable equality
\textbf{f}\equiv\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A}
in
\mathbb{A}
and a context
Y
in
\mathbb{B}
such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}\equiv\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h
. Write
\textbf{Y}=(y_{1}:Y_{1},...,y_{n}:Y_{n})
,
\textbf{f}=(f_{1},...,f_{M})
, and
\textbf{g}=(g_{1},...,g_{M})
. To derive
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}\equiv\textbf{g}\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}
, it suffices to derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash f_{i}\otimes y_{j}\equiv g_{i}\otimes y_{j}\;\mathsf{term}

for
1\leq i\leq M
and
1\leq j\leq n
. But this follows from
h
-derivability by tensoring
\textbf{X}\vdash f_{i}\equiv g_{i}:U
and
\partial_{j-1}\textbf{Y}\vdash Y_{j}\;\mathsf{sort}
where the former is chosen so that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash f_{i}\equiv g_{i}:U)\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}\equiv\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})
.
∎

4.3
Tensoring by a section of a display map

We again work under the assumption that
(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{B})
is
h
-derivable. Suppose given contexts
\textbf{X}=(x_{1}:X_{1},...,x_{m}:X_{m})
in
\mathbb{A}
and
\textbf{Y}=(y_{1}:Y_{1},...,y_{n}:Y_{n})
in
\mathbb{B}
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Consider a derivable judgment
\textbf{X}\vdash u:U
and let
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x:U)
. If
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u:U)\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h
, then we have a context morphism

(x_{1},...,x_{m},u^{U})\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}.

Similarly, consider a derivable judgment
\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V
and let
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y:V)
. If
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V)\leq h
, then we have a context morphism

\textbf{X}\otimes(y_{1},...,y_{n},v^{V}):\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}.

Proposition 4.6
.

Suppose given derivable judgments
\textbf{X}\vdash u:U
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V
such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u:U)\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V)\leq h+1
. Then

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash u^{U}\otimes v^{V}:\overline{\overline{U\otimes V}},\qquad\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash u^{U}\medblackcircle v^{V}:\overline{\overline{U\otimes V}}

are derivable, where the double bar indicates the substitution operation

[u^{U}\otimes y_{1}\mid xy_{1},...,u^{U}\otimes y_{n}\mid xy_{n},x_{1}\otimes v^{V}\mid x_{1}y,...,x_{m}\otimes v^{V}\mid x_{m}y]

for
x
(resp.
y
) a variable of sort
U
(resp.
V
).

Proof.

Firstly, note that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V)<h+1
, so by
h
-derivability we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V
to derive

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x\otimes v^{V}:(U\otimes V)[x_{1}\otimes v^{V}\mid x_{1}y,...,x_{m}\otimes v^{V}\mid x_{m}y]

where
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x:U)
.

Also, by Lemma
4.5
we have a context morphism
(x_{1},...,x_{m},u^{U})\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
. By substitution along this map, we obtain from (
*
) a derivable judgment

\displaystyle\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}

\displaystyle\vdash(x\otimes v^{V})[u^{U}\otimes y_{1}\mid xy_{1},...,u^{U}\otimes y_{n}\mid xy_{n}]

\displaystyle:(U\otimes V)[x_{1}\otimes v^{V}\mid x_{1}y,...,x_{m}\otimes v^{V}\mid x_{m}y][u^{U}\otimes y_{1}\mid xy_{1},...,u^{U}\otimes y_{n}\mid xy_{n}],

which is precisely
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash u^{U}\otimes v^{V}:\overline{\overline{U\otimes V}}
.

Similarly, we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash u:U
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
to derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash u^{U}\otimes y:(U\otimes V)[u^{U}\otimes y_{1}\mid xy_{1},...,u^{U}\otimes y_{n}\mid xy_{n}].

where
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y:V)
. From Lemma
4.5
we obtain a context morphism
\textbf{X}\otimes(y_{1},...,y_{n},v^{V}):\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
, hence by substitution in (
**
) we can derive

\displaystyle\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}

\displaystyle\vdash(u^{U}\otimes y)[x_{1}\otimes v^{V}\mid x_{1}y,...,x_{m}\otimes v^{V}\mid x_{m}y]

\displaystyle:(U\otimes V)[u^{U}\otimes y_{1}\mid xy_{1},...,u^{U}\otimes y_{n}\mid xy_{n}][x_{1}\otimes v^{V}\mid x_{1}y,...,x_{m}\otimes v^{V}\mid x_{m}y],

which equals
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash u^{U}\medblackcircle v^{V}:\overline{\overline{U\otimes V}}
.
∎

Lemma 4.7
.

Suppose given a derivable judgment
\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv v:U
in
\mathbb{A}
such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv v:U)\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h
. Then, writing
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x^{\prime}:U)
, the context morphism equality

(x_{1},...,x_{m},u^{U})\otimes\textbf{Y}\equiv(x_{1},...,x_{m},v^{U})\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}

is derivable.

Proof.

As
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u:U)
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash v:U)\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv v:U)
, by Lemma
4.5
the two premorphisms in the statement are derivable. To obtain the desired morphism equality, it suffices to derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash u^{U}\otimes y_{j}\equiv v^{U}\otimes y_{j}\;\mathsf{term}

for
1\leq j\leq n
. For that, it suffices to derive
\textbf{X}\otimes\partial_{j}\textbf{Y}\vdash u^{U}\otimes y_{j}\equiv v^{V}\otimes y_{j}\;\mathsf{term}
, which is accomplished by tensoring
\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv v:U
and
\partial_{j-1}\textbf{Y}\vdash Y_{j}\;\mathsf{sort}
using
h
-derivability and the fact that
\textsf{ht}(\partial_{j-1}\textbf{Y}\vdash Y_{j}\;\mathsf{sort})\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})
.
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

Suppose given a derivable judgment
\textbf{Y}\vdash u\equiv v:V
in
\mathbb{B}
such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash u\equiv v:V)\leq h
. Then, writing
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y^{\prime}:V)
, the context morphism equality

\textbf{X}\otimes(y_{1},...,y_{n},u)\equiv\textbf{X}\otimes(y_{1},...,y_{n},v):\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}

is derivable.

Proof.

Analogous to Lemma
4.7
.
∎

5
h
-derivability implies
(h+1)
-derivability

This section is devoted to proving the following statement:

Proposition 5.1
.

Given
h\geq 0
, suppose that a pair of generalized algebraic theories
(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{B})
is
h
-derivable. Then it is
(h+1)
-derivable.

It will then be used as the induction step in a proof that the tensor product of any two gats is a theory; see Theorem
5.2
.

In what follows we let
J
and
J^{\prime}
be derivable standard judgments
14
14

14

By a
standard judgment
we mean a sort, term, sort equality or term equality judgment.
in
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{B}
, respectively, that satisfy
\textsf{ht}(J)\textsf{ht}(J^{\prime})=h+1
. Our goal is to prove that
J\odot J^{\prime}
is derivable. This will be done in several steps, according to the kinds of judgment being considered and the rules used to derive them. Note that we can apply to
(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{B})
the results of the previous section, that is,
Cont(k)
,
Sub^{1}_{t,t}(k)
, etc., whenever
k\leq h
.

Below, we always write
\textbf{X}=(x_{1}:X_{1},...,x_{m}:X_{m})
for the context of
J
and
\textbf{Y}=(y_{1}:Y_{1},...,y_{n}:Y_{n})
for that of
J^{\prime}
. We will repeatedly use the fact that
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
is a context, which follows from
Cont(h)
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})<\textsf{ht}(J)\textsf{ht}(J^{\prime})=h+1
.

As in the previous section, we use Proposition
A.15
, without explicit reference, as a source of all properties of the height function that will be referred to.

sort
\odot
sort

Suppose that
J
is
\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
J^{\prime}
is
\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
. Let
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x^{\prime}:X^{\prime})
and
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y^{\prime}:Y^{\prime})
. Then
J\odot J^{\prime}
is

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}.

As
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}^{\prime})
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}^{\prime})
, we have that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}^{\prime})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}^{\prime})
are at most
h
. It follows that
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
and
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
, thus also
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})
, are contexts.

We consider the following (not mutually exclusive) cases:

(1)

Both
J
and
J^{\prime}
are axioms, say
\textbf{X}\vdash S(x_{1},...,x_{m})\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash T(y_{1},...,y_{n})\;\mathsf{sort}
, respectively. Then
J\odot J^{\prime}
is the axiom

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash ST\begin{pmatrix}x_{1}y_{1}&\cdots&x_{1}y_{n}&x_{1}y^{\prime}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
x_{m}y_{1}&\cdots&x_{m}y_{n}&x_{m}y^{\prime}\\
x^{\prime}y_{1}&\cdots&x^{\prime}y_{m}&-\end{pmatrix}\;\mathsf{sort}.

which is derivable as it is an axiom in
\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B}
and
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})
is a context.

(2)

J
has an initial inference of the form (T-sub). Then there exist an axiom
\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
and a morphism
\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A}
such that
X^{\prime}=U[\textbf{f}]
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})
. Let
\textbf{A}^{\prime}
be a context
(\textbf{A},a:U)
.

Note that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})
, so

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}),

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}),

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})

are strictly smaller than
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})=h+1
. This allows us to apply
Sub^{1}_{s,s}(h)
to derive
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
.

(3)

J^{\prime}
has an initial inference of the form (T-sub). Then there exist an axiom
\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
and a morphism
\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B}
such that
Y^{\prime}=V[\textbf{g}]
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})
. Let
\textbf{B}^{\prime}
be a context
(\textbf{B},b:V)
.

Note that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})
, so

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}),

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}),

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})

are strictly smaller than
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})=h+1
. Thus we can apply
Sub^{2}_{s,s}(h)
to derive
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
.

sort
\odot
term

Suppose that
J
is
\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
J^{\prime}
is
\textbf{Y}\vdash v:Y^{\prime}
. Taking contexts
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x^{\prime}:X^{\prime})
and
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y^{\prime}:Y^{\prime})
, recall that
J\odot J^{\prime}
is

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes v:(X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime})[x_{1}\otimes v\mid x_{1}y^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes v\mid x_{m}y^{\prime}].

We have the following cases:

(1)

J
and
J^{\prime}
are axioms, say
\textbf{X}\vdash S(x_{1},...,x_{m})\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash t(y_{1},...,y_{n}):Y^{\prime}
, respectively. Then
J\odot J^{\prime}
is the axiom

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash St\begin{pmatrix}x_{1}y_{1}&\cdots&x_{1}y_{n}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
x_{m}y_{1}&\cdots&x_{m}y_{n}\\
x^{\prime}y_{1}&\cdots&x^{\prime}y_{n}\end{pmatrix}:(X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime})[x_{1}\otimes v\mid x_{1}y^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes v\mid x_{m}y^{\prime}].

For it to be derivable, it must be well-formed, i.e.

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash(X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime})[x_{1}\otimes v\mid x_{1}y^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes v\mid x_{m}y^{\prime}]\;\mathsf{sort}

must be derivable. To check that this is the case, we start by tensoring
\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
to derive

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}.

On the other hand, as
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v:Y^{\prime})\leq h
, by Lemma
4.5
we have a morphism
\textbf{X}\otimes(y_{1},...,y_{n},v):\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
. By amalgamating it with the identity morphism of
\textbf{X}^{\prime}
we derive

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes(y_{1},...,y_{n},v)):\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}).

Now, substitution of (
**
) along the latter morphism yields the desired judgment (
*
).

(2)

J
has an initial inference of the form (T-sub). Then there exist an axiom
\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
and a morphism
\textbf{f}=(f_{1},...,f_{M}):\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A}
such that
X^{\prime}=U[\textbf{f}]
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})
. Let
\textbf{A}^{\prime}
be a context
(\textbf{A},a:U)
. Note that

\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term}),\qquad\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}),\qquad\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term})

are strictly smaller than
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v:Y^{\prime})=h+1
. Thus we can use
Sub^{1}_{s,t}(h)
to derive

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{a\otimes v}}\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes v\;\mathsf{term},

where the double bar indicates substitution along
(f_{1},...,f_{M},x^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{A}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
; the latter being a morphism follows from
Mor^{1}_{+}(h)
.

It remains to show that
x^{\prime}\otimes v
is of the desired sort, that is,

(X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime})[x_{1}\otimes v\mid x_{1}y^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes v\mid x_{m}y^{\prime}].

We will do this by checking that
\overline{\overline{a\otimes v}}
is provably of sort (
\varheartsuit
) in context
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
.

Writing
U=S(a_{1},...,a_{M})
and
Y^{\prime}=T(\tau_{1},...,\tau_{L})
, the matrix form of (
\varheartsuit
) is

ST\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&f_{1}\otimes\tau_{L}&f_{1}\medblackcircle v^{Y^{\prime}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
f_{M}\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&f_{M}\otimes\tau_{L}&f_{M}\medblackcircle v^{Y^{\prime}}\\
x^{\prime}\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&x^{\prime}\otimes\tau_{L}&-\end{pmatrix}.

On the other hand, let us study the sort of
\overline{\overline{a\otimes v}}
. By
h
-derivability,
\textbf{A}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash a\otimes v:(U\otimes Y^{\prime})[a_{1}\otimes v\mid a_{1}y^{\prime},...,a_{M}\otimes v\mid a_{M}y^{\prime}]
is derivable, so we can also derive

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{a\otimes v}}:\overline{\overline{(U\otimes Y^{\prime})[a_{1}\otimes v\mid a_{1}y^{\prime},...,a_{M}\otimes v\mid a_{M}y^{\prime}]}}.

We can express
(U\otimes Y^{\prime})[a_{1}\otimes v\mid a_{1}y^{\prime},...,a_{M}\otimes v\mid a_{M}y^{\prime}]
as

ST\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&a_{1}\otimes\tau_{L}&a_{1}\otimes v\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
a_{M}\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&a_{M}\otimes\tau_{L}&a_{M}\otimes v\\
a\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&a\otimes\tau_{L}&-\end{pmatrix}.

Now, observe that

•

For
1\leq i\leq M
and
w
among
\tau_{1}
, …,
\tau_{L}
, we have
\overline{\overline{a_{i}\otimes w}}=(a_{i}\otimes w)[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}]
, which is provably equal in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
to
f_{i}\otimes w
. This follows from
Sub^{1}_{t,t}(h)
using that

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash w\;\mathsf{term})\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v:Y^{\prime})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash a_{i}\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash w\;\mathsf{term})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v:Y^{\prime})\leq h.

Also, by tensoring
\textbf{X}\vdash f_{i}:\mathcal{S}(f_{i})
(which has height smaller than that of
\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A}
) and
\textbf{Y}\vdash v:Y^{\prime}
we obtain that
f_{i}\otimes v
is provably equal to
f_{i}\medblackcircle v^{V}
.

•

For
1\leq j\leq L
, we can use
Sub^{1}_{s,s}(h)
to derive
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash\overline{\overline{a\otimes\tau_{j}}}\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes\tau_{j}\;\mathsf{term}
.

By applying these modifications to the matrix form of
(U\otimes Y^{\prime})[a_{1}\otimes v\mid a_{1}y^{\prime},...,a_{M}\otimes v\mid a_{M}y^{\prime}]
, we conclude that
\overline{\overline{(U\otimes Y^{\prime})[a_{1}\otimes v\mid a_{1}y^{\prime},...,a_{M}\otimes v\mid a_{M}y^{\prime}]}}
is provably equal to (
\varheartsuit
) in
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
, as required.

(3)

J^{\prime}
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{Y}\vdash V\equiv Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}\quad\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V}{\textbf{Y}\vdash v:Y^{\prime}}\text{(Teq/t)}.

In this case, by
h
-derivability we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V\;\mathsf{sort}
to obtain

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes v:(X^{\prime}\otimes V)[x_{1}\otimes v\mid x_{1}y^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes v\mid x_{m}y^{\prime}].

Hence it suffices to derive

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash(X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime})[x_{1}\otimes v\mid x_{1}y^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes v\mid x_{m}y^{\prime}]\equiv(X^{\prime}\otimes V)[x_{1}\otimes v\mid x_{1}y^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes v\mid x_{m}y^{\prime}]\;\mathsf{sort}.

For that, by tensoring
\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\equiv Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
we derive
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime\prime})\vdash X^{\prime}\otimes V\equiv X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
where
\textbf{Y}^{\prime\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y^{\prime}:V)
. But by
Conteq^{2}(h)
we obtain
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\equiv\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime\prime}\;\textsf{ctx}
, thus also

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash X^{\prime}\otimes V\equiv X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}.

Finally, by Lemma
4.5
(using that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}^{\prime})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V)\leq h
) we can derive
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes(y_{1},...,y_{n},v):\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
, and substitution of (
*
) along this morphism yields the desired sort equality.

(4)

There exists
j\in\{1,...,n\}
such that
Y^{\prime}=Y_{j}
,
v=y_{j}
, and
J^{\prime}
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{Y}\vdash Y_{j}\;\mathsf{sort}}{\textbf{Y}\vdash y_{j}:Y_{j}}\text{(var)}.

By
Cont(h)
we have that
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
is a context. But
(X^{\prime}\otimes Y_{j})[x_{1}y_{j}\mid x_{1}y^{\prime},...,x_{m}y_{j}\mid x_{m}y^{\prime}]
is, by definition, the sort of the variable
x^{\prime}y_{j}
in
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
. An application of (var) then yields
J\odot J^{\prime}
.

(5)

J^{\prime}
has an initial inference (t-sub): there exist an axiom
\textbf{B}\vdash w:W
, where
\textbf{B}=(b_{1}:B_{1},...,b_{N}:B_{N})
, and a morphism
\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B}
such that
Y^{\prime}=W[\textbf{g}]
,
v=w[\textbf{g}]
, and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash w:W)
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v:Y^{\prime})
.

Taking
\textbf{B}^{\prime}=(\textbf{B},b^{\prime}:W)
, we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{B}\vdash w:W
to derive

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{B}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes w:(X^{\prime}\otimes W)[x_{1}\otimes w\mid x_{1}b^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes w\mid x_{m}b^{\prime}].

As
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}^{\prime})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})\leq h
, by
Mor^{2}(h)
we have a morphism
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{B}
. Also, by
Sub^{2}_{s,t}(h)
we obtain

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash(x^{\prime}\otimes w)[\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}]\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes v\;\mathsf{term},

so the claim will follow if we derive

(X^{\prime}\otimes W)[x_{1}\otimes w\mid x_{1}b^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes w\mid x_{m}b^{\prime}][\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}]\equiv(X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime})[x_{1}\otimes v\mid x_{1}y^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes v\mid x_{m}y^{\prime}]

in context
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
.

Writing
X^{\prime}=S(\sigma_{1},...,\sigma_{K})
and
W=T(\tau_{1},...,\tau_{L})
, we have

(X^{\prime}\otimes W)[x_{1}\otimes w\mid x_{1}b^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes w\mid x_{m}b^{\prime}]=ST\begin{pmatrix}\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{L}&\sigma_{1}\medblackcircle w\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\sigma_{K}\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&\sigma_{K}\otimes\tau_{L}&\sigma_{K}\medblackcircle w\\
x^{\prime}\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&x^{\prime}\otimes\tau_{L}&-\end{pmatrix}.

On the other hand,

(X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime})[x_{1}\otimes v\mid x_{1}y^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes v\mid x_{m}y^{\prime}]=ST\begin{pmatrix}\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}[\textbf{g}]&\cdots&\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{L}[\textbf{g}]&\sigma_{1}\medblackcircle v\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\sigma_{K}\otimes\tau_{1}[\textbf{g}]&\cdots&\sigma_{K}\otimes\tau_{L}[\textbf{g}]&\sigma_{K}\medblackcircle v\\
x^{\prime}\otimes\tau_{1}[\textbf{g}]&\cdots&x^{\prime}\otimes\tau_{L}[\textbf{g}]&-\end{pmatrix}.

Now, we obtain (
\varheartsuit
) by deriving an equality, for each entry of the first matrix above, between its pullback along
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}
and the corresponding entry in the second matrix:

•

For
1\leq i\leq K
and
1\leq j\leq L
, we have
(\sigma_{i}\otimes\tau_{j})[\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}]=(\sigma_{i}\otimes\tau_{j})[\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}]
. By
Sub^{2}_{t,s}(h)
, the latter is provably equal to
\sigma_{i}\otimes\tau_{j}[\textbf{g}]
.

•

For
1\leq i\leq K
, we have

\displaystyle(\sigma_{i}\medblackcircle w)[\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}]

\displaystyle=(\sigma_{i}\medblackcircle w)[\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}]

\displaystyle\equiv(\sigma_{i}\otimes w)[\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}]

(
h
-derivability)

\displaystyle\equiv\sigma_{i}\otimes v

(
Sub^{2}_{t,t}(h)
)

\displaystyle\equiv\sigma_{i}\medblackcircle v.

(
h
-derivability)

•

For
1\leq j\leq L
, by
Sub^{2}_{s,t}(h)
we derive
(x^{\prime}\otimes\tau_{j})[\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}]\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes\tau_{j}[\textbf{g}]
in context
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
.

This concludes the proof in the case where
J
is a sort judgment and
J^{\prime}
is a term judgment.

term
\odot
sort

Suppose that
J
is
\textbf{X}\vdash u:X^{\prime}
and
J^{\prime}
is
\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
. Letting
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x^{\prime}:X)
and
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y^{\prime}:Y^{\prime})
, recall that
J\odot J^{\prime}
is

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash u\otimes y^{\prime}:(X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime})[u\otimes y_{1}\mid x^{\prime}y_{1},...,u\otimes y_{n}\mid x^{\prime}y_{n}].

We have the following cases:

(1)

J
and
J^{\prime}
are axioms, say
\textbf{X}\vdash s(x_{1},...,x_{m}):X^{\prime}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash T(y_{1},...,y_{n})\;\mathsf{sort}
, respectively. Then
J\odot J^{\prime}
is the axiom

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\begin{pmatrix}x_{1}y_{1}&\cdots&x_{1}y_{n}&x_{1}y^{\prime}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
x_{m}y_{1}&\cdots&x_{m}y_{n}&x_{m}y^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}(X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime})[u\otimes y_{1}\mid x^{\prime}y_{1},...,u\otimes y_{n}\mid x^{\prime}y_{n}].

For it to be derivable, it must be well-formed, i.e.

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash(X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime})[u\otimes y_{1}\mid x^{\prime}y_{1},...,u\otimes y_{n}\mid x^{\prime}y_{n}]\;\mathsf{sort}

must be derivable. To check that this is the case, we start by tensoring
\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
to derive

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}.

On the other hand, as
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u:X^{\prime})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h
, by Lemma
4.5
we have a morphism
(x_{1},...,x_{m},u):\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
. By amalgamating it with the identity morphism of
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
we derive

\partial((x_{1},...,x_{m},u)\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}):\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}).

Now, substitution of (
**
) along the latter morphism yields (
*
).

(2)

J^{\prime}
has an initial inference of the form (T-sub). Then there exist a sort axiom
\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
and a morphism
\textbf{g}=(g_{1},...,g_{N}):\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B}
such that
Y^{\prime}=V[\textbf{g}]
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})
. Let
\textbf{B}^{\prime}=(\textbf{B},b:V)
. Note that

\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B}),\qquad\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}),\qquad\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})

are strictly smaller than
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u:X^{\prime})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})=h+1
. Thus we can use
Sub^{2}_{t,s}(h)
to derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\overline{\overline{u\otimes v}}\equiv u\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term},

where the double bar indicates substitution along
\textbf{X}\otimes(g_{1},...,g_{N},y^{\prime}):\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\rightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}^{\prime}
; the latter being a morphism follows from
Mor^{2}_{+}(h)
.

It remains to show that
u\otimes y^{\prime}
is of the desired sort, that is,

(X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime})[u\otimes y_{1}\mid x^{\prime}y_{1},...,u\otimes y_{n}\mid x^{\prime}y_{n}].

We will do this by checking that
\overline{\overline{u\otimes b}}
is provably of sort (
\varheartsuit
) in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
.

Writing
X^{\prime}=S(\sigma_{1},...,\sigma_{K})
and
V=T(b_{1},...,b_{N})
, the matrix form of (
\varheartsuit
) is

ST\begin{pmatrix}\sigma_{1}\otimes g_{1}&\cdots&\sigma_{1}\otimes g_{N}&\sigma_{1}\otimes y^{\prime}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\sigma_{K}\otimes g_{1}&\cdots&\sigma_{K}\otimes g_{N}&\sigma_{K}\otimes y^{\prime}\\
u^{X^{\prime}}\otimes g_{1}&\cdots&u^{X^{\prime}}\otimes g_{N}&-\end{pmatrix}.

On the other hand, let us study the sort of
\overline{\overline{u\otimes b}}
. By
h
-derivability,
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}^{\prime}\vdash u\otimes b:(X^{\prime}\otimes V)[u\otimes b_{1}\mid x^{\prime}b_{1},...,u\otimes b_{N}\mid x^{\prime}b_{N}]
is derivable, so we can also derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\overline{\overline{u\otimes b}}:\overline{\overline{(X^{\prime}\otimes V)[u\otimes b_{1}\mid x^{\prime}b_{1},...,u\otimes b_{N}\mid x^{\prime}b_{N}]}}.

We can express
(X^{\prime}\otimes V)[u\otimes b_{1}\mid x^{\prime}b_{1},...,u\otimes b_{N}\mid x^{\prime}b_{N}]
as

ST\begin{pmatrix}\sigma_{1}\otimes b_{1}&\cdots&\sigma_{1}\otimes b_{N}&\sigma_{1}\otimes b\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\sigma_{K}\otimes b_{1}&\cdots&\sigma_{K}\otimes b_{N}&\sigma_{K}\otimes b\\
u\otimes b_{1}&\cdots&u\otimes b_{N}&-\end{pmatrix}.

Now, observe that

•

For
1\leq j\leq N
and
w
among
\sigma_{1}
, …,
\sigma_{K}
, we have
\overline{\overline{w\otimes b_{j}}}=(w\otimes b_{j})[\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}]
, which is provably equal in
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
to
w\otimes g_{j}
. This follows from
Sub^{2}_{t,t}(h)
using that

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash w\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u:X^{\prime})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})\leq h,

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash w\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash b_{j}\;\mathsf{term})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u:X^{\prime})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h.

•

For
1\leq i\leq K
, we can use
Sub^{2}_{s,s}(h)
to derive
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\overline{\overline{\sigma_{i}\otimes b}}\equiv\sigma_{i}\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}
.

By applying these modifications to the matrix form of
(X^{\prime}\otimes V)[u\otimes b_{1}\mid x^{\prime}b_{1},...,u\otimes b_{N}\mid x^{\prime}b_{N}]
, we conclude that
\overline{\overline{(X^{\prime}\otimes V)[u\otimes b_{1}\mid x^{\prime}b_{1},...,u\otimes b_{N}\mid x^{\prime}b_{N}]}}
is provably equal to (
\varheartsuit
) in
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
, as required.

(3)

J
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{X}\vdash U\equiv X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}\quad\textbf{X}\vdash u:U}{\textbf{X}\vdash u:X^{\prime}}\text{(Teq/t)}.

In this case, by
h
-derivability we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash u:U
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
to obtain

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash u\otimes y^{\prime}:(U\otimes Y^{\prime})[u\otimes y_{1}\mid x^{\prime}y_{1},...,u\otimes y_{n}\mid x^{\prime}y_{n}].

Hence it suffices to derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash(X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime})[u\otimes y_{1}\mid x^{\prime}y_{1},...,u\otimes y_{n}\mid x^{\prime}y_{n}]\equiv(U\otimes Y^{\prime})[u\otimes y_{1}\mid x^{\prime}y_{1},...,u\otimes y_{n}\mid x^{\prime}y_{n}]\;\mathsf{sort}.

For that, by tensoring
\textbf{X}\vdash U\equiv X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
we derive
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash U\otimes Y^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
where
\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}=(\textbf{X},x^{\prime},U)
. But by
Conteq^{1}(h)
we obtain
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\equiv\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\;\textsf{ctx}
, thus also

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash U\otimes Y^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}.

Finally, by Lemma
4.5
(using that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u:U)\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\leq h
) we can derive
(x_{1},...,x_{m},u)\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\rightarrow\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
, and substitution of (
*
) along this morphism yields the desired sort equality.

(4)

There exists
i\in\{1,...,m\}
such that
X^{\prime}=X_{i}
,
u=x_{i}
, and
J
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{X}\vdash X_{i}\;\mathsf{sort}}{\textbf{X}\vdash x_{i}:X_{i}}\text{(var)}.

By
Cont(h)
we have that
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
is a context. But
(X_{i}\otimes Y^{\prime})[x_{i}y_{1}\mid x^{\prime}y_{1},...,x_{i}y_{n}\mid x^{\prime}y_{n}]
is, by definition, the sort of the variable
x_{i}y^{\prime}
in
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
. An application of (var) then yields
J\odot J^{\prime}
.

(5)

J
has an initial inference (t-sub): there exist an axiom
\textbf{A}\vdash w:W
, where
\textbf{A}=(a_{1}:A_{1},...,a_{M}:A_{M})
, and a morphism
\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A}
such that
X^{\prime}=W[\textbf{f}]
,
u=w[\textbf{f}]
, and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash w:W)
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u:X^{\prime})
.

Taking
\textbf{A}^{\prime}=(\textbf{A},a^{\prime}:W)
, we can tensor
\textbf{A}\vdash w:W
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
to derive

\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash w\otimes y^{\prime}:(W\otimes Y^{\prime})[w\otimes y_{1}\mid a^{\prime}y_{1},...,w\otimes y_{n}\mid a^{\prime}y_{n}].

As
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\leq h
, by
Mor^{1}(h)
we have a morphism
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\rightarrow\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
. Also, by
Sub^{1}_{t,s}(h)
we obtain

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash(w\otimes y^{\prime})[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}]\equiv u\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term},

so the claim will follow if we derive

(W\otimes Y^{\prime})[w\otimes y_{1}\mid a^{\prime}y_{1},...,w\otimes y_{1}\mid a^{\prime}y_{1}][\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}]\equiv(X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime})[u\otimes y_{1}\mid x^{\prime}y_{1},...,u\otimes y_{n}\mid x^{\prime}y_{n}]

in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
.

Writing
W=S(\sigma_{1},...,\sigma_{K})
and
Y^{\prime}=T(\tau_{1},...,\tau_{L})
, we have

(W\otimes Y^{\prime})[w\otimes y_{1}\mid a^{\prime}y_{1},...,w\otimes y_{n}\mid a^{\prime}y_{n}]=ST\begin{pmatrix}\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{L}&\sigma_{1}\otimes y^{\prime}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\sigma_{K}\otimes\tau_{1}&\dots&\sigma_{K}\otimes\tau_{L}&\sigma_{K}\otimes y^{\prime}\\
w\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&w\otimes\tau_{L}&-\end{pmatrix}.

On the other hand,

(X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime})[u\otimes y_{1}\mid x^{\prime}y_{1},...,u\otimes y_{n}\mid x^{\prime}y_{n}]=ST\begin{pmatrix}\sigma_{1}[\textbf{f}]\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&\sigma_{1}[\textbf{f}]\otimes\tau_{L}&\sigma_{1}[\textbf{f}]\otimes y^{\prime}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\sigma_{K}[\textbf{f}]\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&\sigma_{K}[\textbf{f}]\otimes\tau_{L}&\sigma_{K}[\textbf{f}]\otimes y^{\prime}\\
u\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&u\otimes\tau_{L}&-\end{pmatrix}.

Now, we obtain (
\varheartsuit
) by deriving an equality, for each entry of the first matrix above, between its pullback along
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
and the corresponding entry in the second matrix:

•

For
1\leq i\leq K
and
1\leq j\leq L
, we have
(\sigma_{i}\otimes\tau_{j})[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}]=(\sigma_{i}\otimes\tau_{j})[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}]
. By
Sub^{1}_{s,t}(h)
, the latter is provably equal to
\sigma_{i}[\textbf{f}]\otimes\tau_{j}
.

•

For
1\leq j\leq L
, by
Sub^{1}_{t,t}(h)
we derive
(w\otimes\tau_{j})[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}]\equiv u\otimes\tau_{j}
.

•

For
1\leq i\leq K
, by
Sub^{1}_{t,s}(h)
we derive
(\sigma_{i}\otimes y^{\prime})[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}]\equiv\sigma_{i}[\textbf{f}]\otimes y^{\prime}
.

This concludes the proof in the case where
J
is a term judgment and
J^{\prime}
is a sort judgment.

term
\odot
term

Suppose that
J
is
\textbf{X}\vdash u:U
and
J^{\prime}
is
\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V
. Consider contexts
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x^{\prime}:U)
and
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y^{\prime}:V)
.

Then
J\odot J^{\prime}
is

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash u^{U}\otimes v^{V}\equiv u^{U}\medblackcircle v^{V}:\overline{\overline{U\otimes V}}

where the double bar denotes an application of

[u^{U}\otimes y_{1}\mid x^{\prime}y_{1},...,u^{U}\otimes y_{n}\mid x^{\prime}y_{n},x_{1}\otimes v^{V}\mid x_{1}y^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes v^{V}\mid x_{m}y^{\prime}].

The problem can be simplified as follows: since, by Proposition
4.6
, the judgment
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash u^{U}\otimes v^{V}:\overline{\overline{U\otimes V}}
(as well as
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash u^{U}\medblackcircle v^{V}:\overline{\overline{U\otimes V}}
) is derivable, it suffices to derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash u^{U}\otimes v^{V}\equiv u^{U}\medblackcircle v^{V}\;\mathsf{term}.

Moreover, if
u
or
v
is a variable the claim is trivial since
u^{U}\medblackcircle v^{V}
is defined as
u^{U}\otimes v^{V}
. Thus in what follows we assume that neither
u
nor
v
is a variable. (In particular, we do not consider the cases where
J
or
J^{\prime}
has an initial inference of the form (var).)

We have the following cases:

(1)

J
and
J^{\prime}
are axioms. Then
J\odot J^{\prime}
is an axiom, so its derivability follows from that of the corresponding premises of the rule (t-a), i.e.

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash u\otimes v:\overline{\overline{U\otimes V}},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash u\medblackcircle v:\overline{\overline{U\otimes V}}.

(2)

J
has an initial inference of the form (t-sub). Then there exist an axiom
\textbf{A}\vdash w:W
, where
\textbf{A}=(a_{1}:A_{1},...,a_{M}:A_{M})
, and a morphism
\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A}
such that
U=W[\textbf{f}]
,
u=w[\textbf{f}]
, and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash w:W)
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash u:U)
.

Writing
\textbf{A}^{\prime}
for a context
(\textbf{A},a^{\prime}:W)
, by tensoring
\textbf{A}\vdash w:W
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V
we derive

\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash w\otimes v^{V}\equiv w\medblackcircle v^{V}\;\mathsf{term}.

Moreover, as
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\leq h
, by
Mor^{1}(h)
we have a context morphism
