\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}
. We will verify that
J\odot J^{\prime}
is derivable by describing the pullback along
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}
of the two term expressions in (
*
).

Write
w=s(a_{1},...,a_{M})
,
v=t(t_{1},...,t_{l})
,
W=S(\sigma_{1},...,\sigma_{K})
, and
V=T(\tau_{1},...,\tau_{L})
; also, we use an upper bar (resp. lower bar) to indicate substitution along
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}
(resp. along
f
). Then:

•

w\otimes v^{V}
has matrix form

St\begin{pmatrix}\sigma_{1}\otimes t_{1}&\cdots&\sigma_{1}\otimes t_{l}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\sigma_{K}\otimes t_{1}&\cdots&\sigma_{K}\otimes t_{l}\\
w\otimes t_{1}&\cdots&w\otimes t_{l}\end{pmatrix},

so, by
Sub^{1}_{t,t}(h)
, we have that
\overline{w\otimes v^{V}}
is provably equal in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
to

St\begin{pmatrix}\underline{\sigma_{1}}\otimes t_{1}&\cdots&\underline{\sigma_{1}}\otimes t_{l}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\underline{\sigma_{K}}\otimes t_{1}&\cdots&\underline{\sigma_{K}}\otimes t_{l}\\
\underline{w}\otimes t_{1}&\cdots&\underline{w}\otimes t_{l}\end{pmatrix}=St\begin{pmatrix}\underline{\sigma_{1}}\otimes t_{1}&\cdots&\underline{\sigma_{1}}\otimes t_{l}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\underline{\sigma_{K}}\otimes t_{1}&\cdots&\underline{\sigma_{K}}\otimes t_{l}\\
u\otimes t_{1}&\cdots&u\otimes t_{l}\end{pmatrix}=u\otimes v^{V}.

•

w\medblackcircle v^{V}
has matrix form

sT\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&a_{1}\otimes\tau_{L}&a_{1}\otimes v^{V}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
a_{M}\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&a_{M}\otimes\tau_{L}&a_{M}\otimes v^{V}\end{pmatrix}.

By
Sub^{1}_{t,t}(h)
, it follows that
\overline{w\medblackcircle v^{V}}
is provably equal in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
to

sT\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&f_{1}\otimes\tau_{L}&\overline{a_{1}\otimes v^{V}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
f_{M}\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&f_{M}\otimes\tau_{L}&\overline{a_{M}\otimes v^{V}}\end{pmatrix}.

Now, note that, for
1\leq i\leq M
,

\overline{a_{i}\otimes v^{V}}=\overline{a_{i}\otimes v}\overset{\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}}{\equiv}f_{i}\otimes v=f_{i}\otimes v^{V}\overset{\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}}{\equiv}f_{i}\medblackcircle v^{V},

where the latter equality follows from
h
-derivability. It follows that the latter matrix is provably equal in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
to

sT\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&f_{1}\otimes\tau_{L}&f_{1}\medblackcircle v^{V}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
f_{M}\otimes\tau_{1}&\cdots&f_{M}\otimes\tau_{L}&f_{M}\medblackcircle v^{V}\end{pmatrix}=u\medblackcircle v^{V}.

(3)

The case where
J^{\prime}
has an initial inference of the form (t-sub) can be studied analogously to (2); the verification has been omitted.

(4)

J
has an initial inference of the form (Teq/t), say

\frac{\textbf{X}\vdash U^{\prime}\equiv U\;\mathsf{sort}\quad\textbf{X}\vdash u:U^{\prime}}{\textbf{X}\vdash u:U}.

By
h
-derivability, we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash u:U^{\prime}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V
to derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash u^{U^{\prime}}\otimes v^{V}\equiv u^{U^{\prime}}\medblackcircle v^{V}\;\mathsf{term},

hence it suffices to derive

\displaystyle\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash u^{U^{\prime}}\otimes v^{V}\equiv u^{U}\otimes v^{V}\;\mathsf{term},

(
*
)

\displaystyle\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash u^{U^{\prime}}\medblackcircle v^{V}\equiv u^{U}\medblackcircle v^{V}\;\mathsf{term}.

(
**
)

Recalling that
u
,
v
are assumed to not be variables, we have that
u^{U^{\prime}}\medblackcircle v^{V}
and
u^{U}\medblackcircle v^{V}
are the same expression. Hence it suffices to derive (
*
). For that, we tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash U^{\prime}\equiv U\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V
to derive

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash z^{U^{\prime}}\otimes v^{V}\equiv z^{U}\otimes v^{V}\;\mathsf{term}.

Now, it can be checked that we have an equality of context morphisms

(x_{1},...,x_{m},u^{U^{\prime}})\otimes\textbf{Y}\equiv(x_{1},...,x_{m},u^{U})\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}.

By taking the pullback of the left term expression in (
***
) along
(x_{1},...,x_{m},u^{U^{\prime}})
and of the right one along
(x_{1},...,x_{m},u^{U})
, we obtain

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash(z^{U^{\prime}}\otimes v^{V})[u^{U^{\prime}}\otimes y_{1}\mid zy_{1},...,u^{U^{\prime}}\otimes y_{n}\mid zy_{n}]\equiv(z^{U}\otimes v^{V})[u^{U}\otimes y_{1}\mid zy_{1},...,u^{U}\otimes y_{n}\mid zy_{n}]\;\mathsf{term},

which is precisely (
*
).

(5)

If
J^{\prime}
has an initial inference of the form (Teq/t), the proof is analogous to the one in (4).

sort equality
\odot
sort

Suppose that
J
is
\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
J^{\prime}
is
\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
. Let
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x^{\prime}:X^{\prime})
,
\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}=(\textbf{X},x^{\prime}:X^{\prime\prime})
, and
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y^{\prime}:Y^{\prime})
. Then
J\odot J^{\prime}
is

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}.

Firstly, note that this judgment is well-formed:

•

As
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})
, by
h
-derivability we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
to derive
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
.

•

Similarly, we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
to derive
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime\prime})\vdash X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
. But by
Conteq^{1}(h)
we can derive
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\equiv\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\;\textsf{ctx}
, from which we conclude that
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})
and
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})
are provably equal.

Now, to derive
J\odot J^{\prime}
, we have the following cases:

(1)

J
and
J^{\prime}
are axioms. Then
J\odot J^{\prime}
is derivable as it is well-formed.

(2)

X^{\prime}
equals
X^{\prime\prime}
, and
J
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}}{\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}}(\text{T1}).

Then we can derive
J\odot J^{\prime}
from
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
by using (T1).

(3)

J
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime\prime}\equiv X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}}{\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}}(\text{T2}).

By
h
-derivability, we can tensor the premise and
\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
to derive

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash X^{\prime\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}.

Then we obtain
J\odot J^{\prime}
from
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\equiv\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\;\textsf{ctx}
and an application of (T2).

(4)

J
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}\quad\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime\prime\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime}}{\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}}(\text{T3}).

By
h
-derivability, the judgments
(\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})\otimes J^{\prime}
and
(\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime\prime\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})\otimes J^{\prime}
are derivable. These are, respectively,

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort},

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash X^{\prime\prime\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort},

where
\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}=(\textbf{X},x^{\prime}:X^{\prime\prime\prime})
. Then we obtain
J\odot J^{\prime}
by using
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\equiv\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\;\textsf{ctx}
and an instance of (T3).

(5)

J
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{A}\vdash U\equiv V\;\mathsf{sort}\quad\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}\quad\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}\quad\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A}}{\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}}\text{(Teq-sub-1)}

where
\textbf{A}=(a_{1}:A_{1},...,a_{M}:A_{M})
,
X^{\prime}=U[\textbf{f}]
and
X^{\prime\prime}=V[\textbf{f}]
. By tensoring
\textbf{A}\vdash U\equiv V\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
, we derive

\partial(\textbf{A}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash U\otimes Y^{\prime}\equiv V\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}

where
\textbf{A}^{\prime}=(\textbf{A},a:U)
. By
Mor^{1}_{+}(h)
, we have a morphism
(f_{1},...,f_{M},x^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{A}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
, from which we obtain
\partial((f_{1},...,f_{M},x^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}):\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\rightarrow\partial(\textbf{A}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})
.

By taking the pullback along the latter of (
*
) and applying
Sub^{1}_{s,s}
we derive
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
, as required.

(6)

J
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}\quad\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}\quad\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}\quad\textbf{f}\equiv\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A}}{\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}}\text{(Teq-sub-2)}

where
X^{\prime}=U[\textbf{f}]
and
X^{\prime\prime}=U[\textbf{g}]
. By
h
-normality, we can tensor
\textbf{A}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
to derive

\partial(\textbf{A}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash U\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}

where
\textbf{A}^{\prime}=(\textbf{A},a:U)
. Now, observe that

•

By
Moreq^{1}(h)
we have a morphism equality
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\equiv\textbf{g}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\rightarrow\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
.

•

Let
\textbf{f}=(f_{1},...,f_{M},x^{\prime})
and
\textbf{g}=(g_{1},...,g_{M},x^{\prime})
. Since
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}^{\prime})
, we can use
Mor^{1}_{+}(h)
to obtain morphisms

\textbf{f}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\textbf{A}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y},\quad\quad\quad\quad\textbf{g}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\textbf{A}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}.

But
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
and
\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
are provably equal, so the judgment

\textbf{f}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\equiv\textbf{g}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\textbf{A}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}

is well-formed. In fact, it is derivable: the two maps extend
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}
and
\textbf{g}\otimes\textbf{Y}
, respectively, by the same list of expressions (namely,
x^{\prime}y_{1}
, …,
x^{\prime}y_{n}
).

From the two equalities above we obtain

\partial(\textbf{f}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\equiv\partial(\textbf{g}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}):\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\partial(\textbf{A}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}).

Finally, by taking the pullback of (
*
) along
\partial(\textbf{f}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})
and
\partial(\textbf{g}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})
, and using
Sub^{1}_{s,s}
in each case, we derive

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash U[\textbf{f}]\otimes Y^{\prime}\equiv U[\textbf{g}]\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}.

(7)

J^{\prime}
has an initial inference of the form (T-sub). Then there exist an axiom
\textbf{B}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
, where
\textbf{B}=(b_{1}:B_{1},...,b_{N}:B_{N})
, and a morphism
\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B}
such that
Y^{\prime}=U[\textbf{g}]
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})

\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})
.

By
h
-derivability, we can tensor
J
and

where
\textbf{B}^{\prime}=(\textbf{B},b:U)

•

By
Mor^{2}(h)
, we have a morphism
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{B}
.

•

By
Mor^{2}_{+}(h)
, we have a morphism
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\rightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}^{\prime}
where
\textbf{g}^{\prime}=(g_{1},...,g_{N},y^{\prime})
.

Thus we obtain a map
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime}):\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\rightarrow\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{B}^{\prime})
. With an analogous argument we derive
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime}):\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\rightarrow\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{B}^{\prime})
, and, as the domains (resp. codomains) of these morphisms are provably equal, the judgment

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime})\equiv\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime}):\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{B}^{\prime})

is well-formed. Now, note that the entries in which these two maps differ are of the form
x^{{}^{\prime}X^{\prime}}\otimes g_{i}
and
x^{{}^{\prime}X^{\prime\prime}}\otimes g_{i}
, respectively, for
1\leq i\leq N
. By tensoring
\textbf{X}\vdash X^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash g_{j}:\mathcal{S}(g_{j})
, it follows (using that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash g_{j}:\mathcal{S}(g_{j}))\leq\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})
) that
x^{{}^{\prime}X^{\prime}}\otimes g_{i}\equiv x^{{}^{\prime}X^{\prime\prime}}\otimes g_{i}
in context
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
.

This implies that (
**
) is derivable, and we can take the pullback of the two sort expressions in (
*
) along the two morphisms in (
**
) to derive

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash(X^{\prime}\otimes U)[\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime}]\equiv(X^{\prime\prime}\otimes U)[\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime}]\;\mathsf{sort}.

Now, by
Sub^{2}_{s,s}
we obtain

\displaystyle\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash(X^{\prime}\otimes U)[\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime}]\equiv X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort},

\displaystyle\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash(X^{\prime\prime}\otimes U)[\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime}]\equiv X^{\prime\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort},

and, finally, by replacing the two sort expressions in (
***
) according to the latter equalities we derive the desired judgment
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash X^{\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\equiv X^{\prime\prime}\otimes Y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
.
