sort
\odot
term equality

Suppose that
J
is
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
and
J^{\prime}
is
\textbf{Y}\vdash u\equiv v:V
. Writing
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x^{\prime}:U)
, we want to derive

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes u^{V}\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes v^{V}:(U\otimes V)[x_{1}\otimes u^{V}\mid x_{1}y^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes u^{V}\mid x_{m}y^{\prime}].

To start, note that we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash u:V
to derive

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes u^{V}:(U\otimes V)[x_{1}\otimes u^{V}\mid x_{1}y^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes u^{V}\mid x_{m}y^{\prime}]

where
y^{\prime}
is a variable of sort
V
.

As a consequence, to obtain
J\odot J^{\prime}
it suffices to derive
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes u^{V}\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes v^{V}\;\mathsf{term}
or, equivalently,
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes u\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes v\;\mathsf{term}
.

We have the following cases:

(1)

J
and
J^{\prime}
are axioms. As
J\odot J^{\prime}
is an axiom, to conclude that it is derivable we must verify that it is well-formed; we will do this by checking that the following judgment is derivable:

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash(U\otimes V)[x_{1}\otimes u^{V}\mid x_{1}y^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes u^{V}\mid x_{m}y^{\prime}]\equiv(U\otimes V)[x_{1}\otimes v^{V}\mid x_{1}y^{\prime},...,x_{m}\otimes v^{V}\mid x_{m}y^{\prime}]\;\mathsf{sort}.

By Lemma
4.8
, letting
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y^{\prime}:V)
, we can derive a context morphism equality

\textbf{X}\otimes(y_{1},...,y_{n},u^{V})\equiv\textbf{X}\otimes(y_{1},...,y_{n},v^{V}):\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime},

which in turn yields an equality

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes(y_{1},...,y_{n},u^{V}))\equiv\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes(y_{1},...,y_{n},v^{V})):\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}).

Now, by taking the pullback of
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash U\otimes V\;\mathsf{sort}
along the two morphisms above we obtain (
*
), as required.

(2)

J
has an initial inference of the form (T-sub): there exist an axiom
\textbf{A}\vdash W\;\mathsf{sort}
, say where
\textbf{A}=(a_{1}:A_{1},...,a_{M}:A_{M})
, and a morphism
\textbf{f}=(f_{1},...,f_{M}):\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A}
such that
U=W[\textbf{f}]
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash W\;\mathsf{sort})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})
. Let
\textbf{A}^{\prime}
be a context
(\textbf{A},a^{\prime}:W)
.

By
h
-derivability, we can tensor
\textbf{A}\vdash W\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash u\equiv v:V
to derive

\textbf{A}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash a^{\prime}\otimes u\equiv a^{\prime}\otimes v\;\mathsf{term}.

Now, by using
Sub^{1}_{s,t}(h)
twice we obtain

x^{\prime}\otimes u\equiv\overline{a\otimes u}\equiv\overline{a\otimes u}\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes v

in context
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
, where the upper bar denotes substitution along
(f_{1},...,f_{M},x^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{A}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
.

(3)

J^{\prime}
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{Y}\vdash u:V}{\textbf{Y}\vdash u\equiv u:V}\text{(t1)}.

Then the desired judgment is
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes u\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes u\;\mathsf{term}
, which is derivable by (t1).

(4)

J^{\prime}
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{Y}\vdash v\equiv u:V}{\textbf{Y}\vdash u\equiv v:V}\text{(t2)}.

As
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash v\equiv u:V)\leq h
, we can derive
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes v\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes u\;\mathsf{term}
; we conclude by applying (t2) to the latter judgment.

(5)

J^{\prime}
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{Y}\vdash u\equiv u^{\prime}:V\quad\textbf{Y}\vdash u^{\prime}\equiv v:V}{\textbf{Y}\vdash u\equiv v:V}\text{(t3)}.

By
h
-derivability, we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
with each of the premises to obtain

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes u\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes u^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes u^{\prime}\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes v\;\mathsf{term}.

By applying (t3) we derive
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes u\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes v\;\mathsf{term}
, as required.

(6)

J^{\prime}
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{Y}\vdash V^{\prime}\equiv V\;\mathsf{sort}\quad\textbf{Y}\vdash u\equiv v:V^{\prime}\quad\textbf{Y}\vdash u:V\quad\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V}{\textbf{Y}\vdash u\equiv v:V}\text{(Teq/teq)}.

By
h
-derivability, we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash u\equiv v:V^{\prime}
to obtain

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes u^{V^{\prime}}\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes v^{V^{\prime}}\;\mathsf{term}.

This is the desired judgment as
x^{\prime}\otimes u^{V^{\prime}}=x^{\prime}\otimes u
and
x^{\prime}\otimes v^{V^{\prime}}=x^{\prime}\otimes v
.

(7)

J^{\prime}
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{B}\vdash\alpha\equiv\beta:Z\quad\textbf{Y}\vdash u:V\quad\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V\quad\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B}}{\textbf{Y}\vdash u\equiv v:V}\text{(teq-sub-1)}

where
u=\alpha[\textbf{g}]
,
v=\beta[\textbf{g}]
, and
V=Z[\textbf{g}]
. By
h
-derivability, we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{B}\vdash\alpha\equiv\beta:Z
to derive

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{B}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes\alpha\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes\beta\;\mathsf{term}.

Noting that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash\alpha\;\mathsf{term})
are at most
h
, by
Sub^{2}_{s,t}(h)
we can derive
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash(x^{\prime}\otimes\alpha)[\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}]\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes u\;\mathsf{term}
. Similarly we derive
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash(x^{\prime}\otimes\beta)[\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}]\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes v\;\mathsf{term}
, from which it follows that

x^{\prime}\otimes u\equiv(x^{\prime}\otimes\alpha)[\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}]\equiv(x^{\prime}\otimes\beta)[\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}]\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes v

in context
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
.

(8)

J^{\prime}
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{B}\vdash\alpha:Z\quad\textbf{Y}\vdash u:V\quad\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V\quad\textbf{f}\equiv\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B}}{\textbf{Y}\vdash u\equiv v:V}

where
u=\alpha[\textbf{f}]
,
v=\alpha[\textbf{g}]
, and
V=Z[\textbf{f}]
.

By tensoring
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{B}\vdash\alpha:Z
we derive
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{B}\vdash x^{\prime}\otimes\alpha\;\mathsf{term}
. On the other hand, as
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}^{\prime})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}\equiv\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})\leq h
, by
Moreq^{2}(h)
we derive
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{f}\equiv\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{B}
.

It follows that

x^{\prime}\otimes u\equiv(x^{\prime}\otimes\alpha)[\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{f}]\equiv(x^{\prime}\otimes\alpha)[\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}]\equiv x^{\prime}\otimes v

in context
\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
, where the middle equality is obtained by substitution, and the first and third ones by
Mor^{2}_{s,t}(h)
.

This concludes the proof in the case where
J
is a sort judgment and
J^{\prime}
is a term equality judgment.

term equality
\odot
sort

The proof is similar to the previous one. Suppose that
J
is
\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv v:U
and
J^{\prime}
is
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
. Writing
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y^{\prime}:V)
, we want to derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash u\otimes y^{\prime}\equiv v\otimes y^{\prime}:(U\otimes V)[u\otimes y_{1}\mid x^{\prime}y_{1},...,u\otimes y_{n}\mid x^{\prime}y_{n}]

where
x^{\prime}
is a variable of sort
U
.

By tensoring
\textbf{X}\vdash u:U
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
we derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash u\otimes y^{\prime}:(U\otimes V)[u\otimes y_{1}\mid x^{\prime}y_{1},...,u\otimes y_{n}\mid x^{\prime}y_{n}],

so to obtain
J\odot J^{\prime}
it suffices to derive
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash u\otimes y^{\prime}\equiv v\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}
. We have the following cases:

(1)

J
and
J^{\prime}
are axioms. To derive
J\odot J^{\prime}
, which is an axiom, we must verify that it is well-formed; for that, we will derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash(U\otimes V)[u\otimes y_{1}\mid x^{\prime}y_{1},...,u\otimes y_{n}\mid x^{\prime}y_{n}]\equiv(U\otimes V)[v\otimes y_{1}\mid x^{\prime}y_{1},...,v\otimes y_{n}\mid x^{\prime}y_{n}]\;\mathsf{sort}.

By Lemma
4.7
, letting
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x:U)
, we can derive a morphism equality

(x_{1},...,x_{m},u)\otimes\textbf{Y}\equiv(x_{1},...,x_{m},v):\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\longrightarrow\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y},

hence also

\partial((x_{1},...,x_{m},u)\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\equiv\partial((x_{1},...,x_{m},v)\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}):\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}).

The desired judgment (
*
) is now obtained by substitution of
\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash U\otimes V\;\mathsf{sort}
along the two morphisms above.

(2)

J^{\prime}
has an initial inference of the form (T-sub). Then there exist an axiom
\textbf{B}\vdash W\;\mathsf{sort}
, say where
\textbf{B}=(b_{1}:B_{1},...,b_{N}:B_{N})
, and a morphism
\textbf{g}=(g_{1},...,g_{N}):\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B}
such that
V=W[\textbf{g}]
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B})
,
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{B}\vdash W\;\mathsf{sort})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
. Let
\textbf{B}^{\prime}
be a context
(\textbf{B},b^{\prime}:W)
.

By
h
-derivability, we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv v:U
and
\textbf{B}\vdash W\;\mathsf{sort}
to derive

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}^{\prime}\vdash u\otimes b^{\prime}\equiv v\otimes b^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}.

We conclude by using
Sub^{2}_{t,s}
twice to obtain

u\otimes y^{\prime}\equiv\overline{u\otimes b^{\prime}}\equiv\overline{v\otimes b^{\prime}}\equiv v\otimes y^{\prime}

in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
, where the upper bar denotes substitution along
\textbf{X}\otimes(g_{1},...,g_{N},y^{\prime}):\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\rightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}^{\prime}
.

(3)

J
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{X}\vdash u:U}{\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv u:U}\text{(t1)}.

Then the desired judgment is
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash u\otimes y^{\prime}\equiv u\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}
, which is derivable by (t1).

(4)

J
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{X}\vdash v\equiv u:V}{\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv v:V}\text{(t2)}.

As
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash v\equiv u:V)\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})\leq h
, we can derive
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash u\otimes y^{\prime}\equiv v\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}
; we conclude by applying (t2) to the latter judgment.

(5)

J
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv u^{\prime}:U\quad\textbf{X}\vdash u^{\prime}\equiv v:V}{\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv v:V}\text{(t3)}.

By
h
-derivability, we can tensor each of the premises with
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
to obtain

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash u\otimes y^{\prime}\equiv u^{\prime}\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash u^{\prime}\otimes y^{\prime}\equiv v\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}.

By applying (t3) we derive
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash u\otimes y^{\prime}\equiv v\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}
, as required.

(6)

J
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{X}\vdash U^{\prime}\equiv U\;\mathsf{sort}\quad\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv v:U^{\prime}\quad\textbf{X}\vdash u:U\quad\textbf{X}\vdash v:U}{\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv v:U}\text{(Teq/teq)}.

By
h
-derivability, we can tensor
\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv v:U^{\prime}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
to obtain

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash u^{U^{\prime}}\otimes y^{\prime}\equiv v^{U^{\prime}}\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}.

This is the desired judgment as
u^{U^{\prime}}\otimes y^{\prime}=u\otimes y^{\prime}
and
v^{U^{\prime}}\otimes y^{\prime}=v\otimes y^{\prime}
.

(7)

J
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{A}\vdash\alpha\equiv\beta:Z\quad\textbf{X}\vdash u:U\quad\textbf{X}\vdash v:U\quad\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A}}{\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv v:U}\text{(teq-sub-1)}

where
u=\alpha[\textbf{f}]
,
v=\beta[\textbf{g}]
, and
U=Z[\textbf{f}]
. By
h
-derivability, we can tensor
\textbf{A}\vdash\alpha\equiv\beta:Z
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
to derive

\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\alpha\otimes y^{\prime}\equiv\beta\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}.

Noting that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
and
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{A}\vdash\alpha\;\mathsf{term})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
are at most
h
, by
Sub^{1}_{t,s}(h)
we can derive
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash(\alpha\otimes y^{\prime})[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}]\equiv u\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}
. Similarly we derive
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash(\beta\otimes y^{\prime})[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}]\equiv v\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}
, from which it follows that

u\otimes y^{\prime}\equiv(\alpha\otimes y^{\prime})[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}]\equiv(\beta\otimes y^{\prime})[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}]\equiv v\otimes y^{\prime}

in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
.

(8)

J
has an initial inference

\frac{\textbf{A}\vdash\alpha:Z\quad\textbf{X}\vdash u:U\quad\textbf{X}\vdash v:U\quad\textbf{f}\equiv\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A}}{\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv v:U}

where
u=\alpha[\textbf{f}]
,
v=\alpha[\textbf{g}]
, and
U=Z[\textbf{f}]
.

By tensoring
\textbf{A}\vdash\alpha:Z
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
we derive
\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash\alpha\otimes y^{\prime}\;\mathsf{term}
. On the other hand, as
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{f}\equiv\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\leq h
, by
Moreq^{1}(h)
we derive
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\equiv\textbf{g}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\rightarrow\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
.

It follows that

u\otimes y^{\prime}\equiv(\alpha\otimes y^{\prime})[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}]\equiv(\alpha\otimes y^{\prime})[\textbf{g}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}]\equiv v\otimes y^{\prime}

in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}
, where the middle equality is obtained by substitution, and the first and third ones by
Sub^{1}_{t,s}(h)
.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.1
. By induction, we obtain the main result of the text:

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{B}
be generalized algebraic theories. Then
(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{B})
is
h
-derivable for all
h\geq 0
. In particular, the pretheory
\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B}
is a theory.

6
Two-sided substitution and the functor
\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A})\times\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{B})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})

Let
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{B}
be generalized algebraic theories. We have proved (Theorem
5.2
) that
\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B}
is a theory; in fact, that
J\odot J^{\prime}
is derivable whenever
J
,
J^{\prime}
are derivable judgments in
\mathbb{A}
,
\mathbb{B}
, respectively. Also, all the statements from §
4
hold without reference to the parameter
h
; we will thus say, for example, that
(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{B})
satisfies
Sub^{1}_{t,t}
, meaning that it satisfies
Sub^{1}_{t,t}(h)
for all
h\geq 0
, and similarly for the other conditions in that section.

We will now consider tensor products of context morphisms and describe a form of commutativity between such tensor products and the substitution operation (which we refer to as “two-sided substitution”). Using that, we will define a comparison functor
\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A})\times\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{B})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})
between the corresponding contextual categories.

We refer the reader to
[
11
]
(and, for more detail, to
[
10
]
) for some aspects of generalized algebraic theories not covered in the appendix (and which do not depend on the differences between Cartmell’s presentation and ours). Notably, we have a category
GAT
of generalized algebraic theories and interpretations, and a category
Cont
of contextual categories and contextual functors.

Each theory
\mathbb{A}
has an associated contextual category
\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A})
where, in particular, objects are equivalence classes (with respect to provable equality) of contexts, and whose arrows are equivalence classes of context morphisms. These assemble into a functor

\mathcal{C}:\text{GAT}\longrightarrow\text{Cont},

which is proved in
[
10
]
to be an equivalence of categories.

Given a context
X
in
\mathbb{A}
, the corresponding object in
\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A})
will be denoted by
[\textbf{X}]
. Similarly, for a context morphism
\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{Y}
we write
[\textbf{f}]
for the induced arrow from
[\textbf{X}]
to
[\textbf{Y}]
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Suppose given context morphisms

\textbf{f}=(f_{1},...,f_{M}):\textbf{X}=(x_{1}:X_{1},...,x_{m}:X_{m})\longrightarrow\textbf{A}=(a_{1}:A_{1},...,a_{M}:A_{M})

in
\mathbb{A}
and

\textbf{g}=(g_{1},...,g_{N}):\textbf{Y}=(y_{1}:Y_{1},...,y_{n}:Y_{n})\longrightarrow\textbf{B}=(b_{1}:B_{1},...,b_{N}:B_{N})

in
\mathbb{B}
. Then
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{g}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{B}
is derivable. Moreover, it is equal to both composites in the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{Y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{B}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}}

Here, we have used
Mor^{1}
and
Mor^{2}
to conclude that these four sequences are morphisms with the indicated co/domains.

Proof.

We check that the entries of
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{g}
are provably equal in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
to the corresponding entries in
(\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{g})\circ(\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y})
and, similarly, in
(\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{B})\circ(\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g})
. Either statement implies, in particular, that
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{g}
is a morphism from
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
to
\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{B}
.

Let
1\leq i\leq M
and
1\leq j\leq N
. The
(i,j)
-entry of the matrix form of
(\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{g})\circ(\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y})
is
(a_{i}\otimes g_{j})[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}]
. By
Sub^{1}_{t,t}
, the latter is provably equal in
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
to
f_{i}\otimes g_{j}
, which is the
(i,j)
-entry of
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{g}
.

The other case follows similarly from
Sub^{2}_{t,t}
.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

Consider context morphisms

\textbf{X}\overset{\textbf{f}}{\longrightarrow}\textbf{X}^{\prime}\overset{\textbf{f}^{\prime}}{\longrightarrow}\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime},\qquad\qquad\qquad\textbf{Y}\overset{\textbf{g}}{\longrightarrow}\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\overset{\textbf{g}^{\prime}}{\longrightarrow}\textbf{Y}^{\prime\prime}

in
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{B}
, respectively. Then the composites

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\overset{\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}}{\longrightarrow}\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\overset{\textbf{f}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}}{\longrightarrow}\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y},\qquad\qquad\qquad\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\overset{\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}}{\longrightarrow}\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\overset{\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime}}{\longrightarrow}\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime\prime}

are provably equal in
\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B}
to
(\textbf{f'}\circ\textbf{f})\otimes\textbf{Y}:\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}
and to
\textbf{X}\otimes(\textbf{g}^{\prime}\circ\textbf{g}):\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime\prime}
, respectively.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of the previous lemma: the
(i,j)
-entry of the left-hand side composite is
(f^{\prime}_{i}\otimes y_{j})[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}]
, which by
Sub^{1}_{t,t}
is provably equal in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
to
f^{\prime}_{i}[\textbf{f}]\otimes y_{j}=(\textbf{f}^{\prime}\circ\textbf{f})_{i}\otimes y_{j}
. The second case is verified similarly from
Sub^{2}_{t,t}
.
∎

Proposition 6.3
.

In the setting of Lemma
6.2
, the morphisms

(\textbf{f}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime})\circ(\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{g}),\qquad\qquad(\textbf{f}^{\prime}\circ\textbf{f})\otimes(\textbf{g}^{\prime}\circ\textbf{g})

from
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
to
\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime\prime}
are provably equal.

Proof.

By the two lemmas above, we have a diagram of context morphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\textbf{f}^{\prime}\circ\textbf{f})\otimes\textbf{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\otimes g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textbf{f}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes(\textbf{g}^{\prime}\circ\textbf{g})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textbf{f}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}\otimes g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime\prime}}

that commutes up to provable equality. Also, by Lemma
6.1
we can derive a morphism equality

\big(\textbf{X}^{\prime\prime}\otimes(\textbf{g}^{\prime}\circ\textbf{g})\big)\circ\big((\textbf{f}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{f})\otimes\textbf{Y}\big)\equiv(\textbf{g}^{\prime}\circ\textbf{g})\otimes(\textbf{f}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{f}).

∎

Proposition 6.4

(Two-sided substitution)
.

Suppose given a derivable judgment
\textbf{A}\vdash u\;\mathsf{term}
and a context morphism
\textbf{f}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A}
in
\mathbb{A}
and, similarly,
\textbf{B}\vdash v\;\mathsf{term}
and
\textbf{g}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B}
in
\mathbb{B}
. Then the judgment

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash(u\otimes v)[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{g}]\equiv u[\textbf{f}]\otimes v[\textbf{g}]\;\mathsf{term}

is derivable.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.1
,
Sub^{1}
and
Sub^{2}
, we have the following derivable equalities in context
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
:

\displaystyle(u\otimes v)[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{g}]

\displaystyle\equiv(u\otimes v)[(\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{B})\circ(\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g})]

\displaystyle\equiv(u\otimes v)[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{B}][\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}]

\displaystyle\equiv(u[\textbf{f}]\otimes v)[\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{g}]

\displaystyle\equiv u[\textbf{f}]\otimes v[\textbf{g}].

∎

Construction 6.5
.

We define a functor
\otimes_{\mathbb{A},\mathbb{B}}:\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A})\times\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{B})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})
, also denoted by
\otimes
by abuse of notation, as follows:

•

Given derivable equalities
\textbf{X}\equiv\textbf{X}^{\prime}\;\textsf{ctx}
in
\mathbb{A}
and
\textbf{Y}\equiv\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\;\textsf{ctx}
in
\mathbb{B}
, from
Conteq^{1}
and
Conteq^{2}
we obtain

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\equiv\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\equiv\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}.

We then let
[\textbf{X}]\otimes[\textbf{Y}]=[\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}]
.

•

Given derivable equalities
\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{f}^{\prime}:\textbf{X}\rightarrow\textbf{A}
in
\mathbb{A}
and
\textbf{g}\equiv\textbf{g}^{\prime}:\textbf{Y}\rightarrow\textbf{B}
in
\mathbb{B}
, we obtain an equality

\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{g}\equiv(\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{g})\circ(\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{Y})\equiv(\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{g}^{\prime})\circ(\textbf{f}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y})\equiv\textbf{f}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{g}

between morphisms from
\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}
to
\textbf{A}\otimes\textbf{B}
by using
Moreq^{1}
,
Moreq^{2}
and Proposition
6.1
.

We let
[\textbf{f}]\otimes[\textbf{g}]:[\textbf{X}]\otimes[\textbf{Y}]\rightarrow[\textbf{A}]\otimes[\textbf{B}]
be
[\textbf{f}\otimes\textbf{g}]
.

•

For functionality, note firstly that the equality
(x_{1},...,x_{m})\otimes(y_{1},...,y_{n})=(x_{1}y_{1},...,x_{m}y_{n})
yields
id_{[\textbf{X}]}\otimes id_{[\textbf{Y}]}=id_{[\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}]}=id_{[\textbf{X}]\otimes[\textbf{Y}]}
. On the other hand, preservation of composition follows from Proposition
6.3
.

Remark 6.6
.

Suppose that we extend
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{B}
to theories
\mathbb{A}^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{B}^{\prime}
, respectively, by adding a set of axioms (including, possibly, new symbols). It can be checked by induction on the height that all the operations of the form
-\otimes-
between structures in
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{B}
(such as tensoring two judgments, contexts, or context morphisms) are identical to the restrictions to structures in
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{B}
of the corresponding operations for
\mathbb{A}^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{B}^{\prime}
. This implies a weak form of naturality of the functors given by Construction
6.5
: the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A})\times\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A}^{\prime})\times\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{B}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{B}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes_{\mathbb{A},\mathbb{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(I)\times\mathcal{C}(I)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes_{\mathbb{A}^{\prime},\mathbb{B}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(I)}

commutes where
I
is, in each case, the identity interpretation.

Remark 6.7
.

Consider the following statement: given morphisms of theories
F:\mathbb{A}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{\prime}
and
G:\mathbb{B}\rightarrow\mathbb{B}^{\prime}
, there exists a unique contextual functor
H:\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{B}^{\prime})
such that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A})\times\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A}^{\prime})\times\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{B}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A}^{\prime}\otimes\mathbb{B}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes_{\mathbb{A},\mathbb{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}(F)\times\mathcal{C}(G)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes_{\mathbb{A}^{\prime},\mathbb{B}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}

commutes. This would immediately turn the tensor product of theories into a functor
\otimes:\text{GAT}\times\text{GAT}\rightarrow\text{GAT}
. However, giving a purely syntactic proof of the above statement seems to be a laborious task due to the recursive nature of morphisms of gats (as equivalence classes of interpretations) is; compare, for example, with the definition of a contextual functor. We will come back to this discussion in
[
4
]
.

7
Towards associativity

In this section we outline a description of an isomorphism
(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})\otimes\mathbb{C}\cong\mathbb{A}\otimes(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{C})
for arbitrary gats
\mathbb{A}
,
\mathbb{B}
,
\mathbb{C}
. (But we only see this as associativity in a restricted sense; see Remark
7.5
.)

To start, observe that the tensor product of sort-and-term alphabets from Definition
2.1
is associative. Given
\Sigma_{1}
,
\Sigma_{2}
and
\Sigma_{3}
, the alphabet
(\Sigma_{1}\otimes\Sigma_{2})\otimes\Sigma_{3}
has a set of variables
(\Sigma_{1}^{var}\times\Sigma_{2}^{var})\times\Sigma_{3}^{var}
, a set of sort symbols
(\Sigma_{1}^{sort}\times\Sigma_{2}^{sort})\times\Sigma_{3}^{sort}
, and a set of term symbols
(\Sigma_{1}\otimes\Sigma_{2})^{sort}\times\Sigma_{3}^{term}\;\;\sqcup\;\;(\Sigma_{1}\otimes\Sigma_{2})^{term}\times\Sigma_{3}^{sort}
, which equals

(\Sigma_{1}^{sort}\times\Sigma_{2}^{sort})\times\Sigma_{3}^{term}\;\;\sqcup\;\;(\Sigma_{1}^{sort}\times\Sigma_{2}^{term}\sqcup\Sigma_{1}^{term}\times\Sigma_{2}^{sort})\times\Sigma_{3}^{term}.

Similarly,
\Sigma_{1}\otimes(\Sigma_{2}\otimes\Sigma_{3})
has a set of variables
\Sigma_{1}^{var}\times(\Sigma_{2}^{var}\times\Sigma_{3}^{var})
, a set of sort symbols
\Sigma_{1}^{sort}\times(\Sigma_{2}^{sort}\times\Sigma_{3}^{sort})
, and a sort of term symbols
\Sigma_{1}^{sort}\times(\Sigma_{2}^{sort}\times\Sigma_{3}^{term}\sqcup\Sigma_{2}^{term}\times\Sigma_{3}^{sort})\;\;\sqcup\;\;\Sigma_{1}^{term}\times(\Sigma_{2}^{sort}\times\Sigma_{3}^{sort})
. Each of the three sets in the first case is canonically isomorphic to the corresponding set in the second one; hence we will identify both alphabets with the alphabet
\Sigma_{1}\otimes\Sigma_{2}\otimes\Sigma_{3}
defined as having a set of variables
\Sigma_{1}^{var}\times\Sigma_{2}^{var}\times\Sigma_{3}^{var}
, a set of sort symbols
\Sigma_{1}^{sort}\times\Sigma_{2}^{sort}\times\Sigma_{3}^{sort}
, and a set of term symbols

(\Sigma_{1}^{sort}\times\Sigma_{2}^{sort}\times\Sigma_{3}^{term})\sqcup(\Sigma_{1}^{sort}\times\Sigma_{2}^{term}\times\Sigma_{3}^{sort})\sqcup(\Sigma_{1}^{term}\times\Sigma_{2}^{sort}\times\Sigma_{3}^{sort}).

We will denote a triple in one of these sets by concatenating the entries; for example, given
R\in\Sigma_{1}^{sort}
,
S\in\Sigma_{2}^{sort}
and
t\in\Sigma_{3}^{term}
, we write
RSt
for
(R,S,t)
.

For theories
\mathbb{A}
,
\mathbb{B}
and
\mathbb{C}
, we will abuse notation and view
(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})\otimes\mathbb{C}
and
\mathbb{A}\otimes(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{C})
as having the same alphabet, namely,
\Sigma(\mathbb{A})\otimes\Sigma(\mathbb{B})\otimes\Sigma(\mathbb{C})
. More precisely, we will also write
(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})\otimes\mathbb{C}
and
\mathbb{A}\otimes(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{C})
for the theories in the alphabet
\Sigma(\mathbb{A})\otimes\Sigma(\mathbb{B})\otimes\Sigma(\mathbb{C})
obtained by translating the respective sets of axioms (hence of derivable judgments) along the canonical bijections between sets of symbols. Under this convention, our goal will be to prove that these two theories have the same derivable judgments.

7.1
Preliminary notations

Before discussing the associativity of the tensor product of theories, we introduce a syntactic structure that will be useful for describing the tensor product of a sequence of expressions coming from two or more theories.

Notation 7.1
.

Let
\mathbb{A}
be a theory. For a derivable judgment
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
, where
U=T(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{k})
, and a variable
x
not occurring in
X
, we let
U\{x\}
be the pair
(T(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{k},x),\;\textbf{X})
. We view
X
as a label on the expression
T(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{k},x)
that, in particular, endows each variable occurring in it with a sort: the variables in
\alpha_{1}
, …,
\alpha_{k}
have the sorts specified by
X
, and
x
has sort
U
.

Similarly, for derivable
\textbf{X}\vdash u:U
we write
U\{u\}
for
(T(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{k},u),\;\textbf{X})
.

In either case, we omit the context
X
from the notation when it is implicit.

Note that
U
can be recovered from
U\{x\}=T(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{k},x)
or
U\{u\}=T(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{k},u)
by removing the last entry between parentheses. However, it will be useful for performing calculations to denote
U\{x\}
(resp.
U\{u\}
) by
A(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{k},x^{U})
(resp.
A(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{k},u^{U})
).

The usefulness of this notation comes from the fact that, in many cases, such expressions can be naturally used to describe the operations introduced in §
2
. To understand that, we introduce a further set of notations.

Let
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{B}
be theories, and consider expressions
U\{u\}
,
V\{v\}
of one of the two forms described above with respect to
\mathbb{A}
,
\mathbb{B}
, respectively. That is, we are given

•

in
\mathbb{A}
, either a derivable judgment
\textbf{X}\vdash u:U
, or a derivable judgment
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
such that
u
is the chosen variable not occurring in
X
; and

•

in
\mathbb{B}
, either a derivable judgment
\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V
, or a derivable judgment
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
such that
v
is the chosen variable not in
Y
.

Write
U=R(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{M})
and
V=S(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{N})
. Then
U\{u\}=R(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{m},u^{U})
and
V\{v\}=S(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{n},v^{V})
, and we define their “box product” as

U\{u\}\square V\{v\}:=RS((\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{m},u^{U})\otimes(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{N},v^{V}))=RS\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&\alpha_{1}\otimes\beta_{N}&\alpha_{1}\otimes v^{V}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\alpha_{M}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&\alpha_{M}\otimes\beta_{N}&\alpha_{M}\otimes v^{V}\\
u^{U}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&u^{U}\otimes\beta_{N}&u^{U}\otimes v^{V}\end{pmatrix}.

We also consider the following variants, where we replace some instances of
\otimes
by
\medblackcircle
:

\displaystyle U\{u\}\medblacksquare V\{v\}:=RS\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&\alpha_{1}\otimes\beta_{N}&\alpha_{1}\medblackcircle v^{V}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\alpha_{M}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&\alpha_{M}\otimes\beta_{N}&\alpha_{M}\medblackcircle v^{V}\\
u^{U}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&u^{U}\otimes\beta_{N}&u^{U}\medblackcircle v^{V}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle U\{u\}\mathrel{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to5.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.75pt\lower-2.75pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 3.46 M -3.46 3.46 L -3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 3.46 Z M 3.46 -3.46}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 0 M -3.46 0 L -3.46 3.46 L 3.46 3.46 L 3.46 0 Z M 3.46 3.46}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}V\{v\}:=RS\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&\alpha_{1}\otimes\beta_{N}&\alpha_{1}\medblackcircle v^{V}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\alpha_{M}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&\alpha_{M}\otimes\beta_{N}&\alpha_{M}\medblackcircle v^{V}\\
u^{U}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&u^{U}\otimes\beta_{N}&u^{U}\otimes v^{V}\end{pmatrix}.

Similarly, if
u
is not a variable, say
u=s(a_{1},...,a_{m})
, we let

\displaystyle u\square V\{v\}

\displaystyle:=rS((a_{1},...,a_{m})\otimes(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{N},v^{V}))=rS\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&a_{1}\otimes\beta_{N}&a_{1}\otimes v^{V}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
a_{m}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&a_{m}\otimes\beta_{N}&a_{m}\otimes v^{V}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle u\medblacksquare V\{v\}

\displaystyle:=rS((a_{1},...,a_{m})\medblackcircle(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{N},v^{V})):=rS\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&a_{1}\otimes\beta_{N}&a_{1}\medblackcircle v^{V}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
a_{m}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&a_{m}\otimes\beta_{N}&a_{m}\medblackcircle v^{V}\end{pmatrix},

and when
v
is not a variable, say
v=s(b_{1},...,b_{n})
, we let

\displaystyle U\{u\}\square v

\displaystyle:=Rs((\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{m},u^{U})\otimes(b_{1},...,b_{n}))=Rs\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\otimes b_{1}&\cdots&\alpha_{1}\otimes b_{n}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\alpha_{M}\otimes b_{1}&\cdots&\alpha_{M}\otimes b_{n}\\
u^{U}\otimes b_{1}&\cdots&u^{U}\otimes b_{n}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle U\{u\}\medblacksquare v

\displaystyle:=Rs((\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{m},u^{U})\medblackcircle(b_{1},...,b_{n})):=Rs\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\otimes b_{1}&\cdots&\alpha_{1}\medblackcircle b_{n}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\alpha_{M}\otimes b_{1}&\cdots&\alpha_{M}\medblackcircle b_{n}\\
u^{U}\otimes b_{1}&\cdots&u^{U}\medblackcircle b_{n}\end{pmatrix}.

These expressions allow us to present tensor products between sorts and terms in a way that can be easily iterated. For example, if
u
and
v
are not variables, then

u^{U}\otimes v^{V}=U\{u\}\square v,\qquad\qquad u^{U}\medblackcircle v^{V}=u\medblacksquare V\{v\},

and the tensor product of
\textbf{X}\vdash u:U
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V
equals

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash U\{u\}\square v\equiv u\medblacksquare V\{v\}:\partial(U\{u\}\mathrel{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to5.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.75pt\lower-2.75pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 3.46 M -3.46 3.46 L -3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 3.46 Z M 3.46 -3.46}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 0 M -3.46 0 L -3.46 3.46 L 3.46 3.46 L 3.46 0 Z M 3.46 3.46}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}V\{v\}).

Remark 7.2
.

Denoting by
K
the sort
\partial(U\{u\}\mathrel{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to5.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.75pt\lower-2.75pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 3.46 M -3.46 3.46 L -3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 3.46 Z M 3.46 -3.46}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 0 M -3.46 0 L -3.46 3.46 L 3.46 3.46 L 3.46 0 Z M 3.46 3.46}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}V\{v\})=\partial(U\{u\}\medblacksquare V\{v\})
in the above judgment, we have

K\{u^{U}\otimes v^{V}\}=U\{u\}\mathrel{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to5.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.75pt\lower-2.75pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 3.46 M -3.46 3.46 L -3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 3.46 Z M 3.46 -3.46}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 0 M -3.46 0 L -3.46 3.46 L 3.46 3.46 L 3.46 0 Z M 3.46 3.46}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}V\{v\},\qquad\qquad K\{u^{U}\medblackcircle v^{V}\}=U\{u\}\medblacksquare V\{v\}.

This exemplifies a general compatibility, which will be the key for verifying associativity of the tensor product of theories, between the box product and the tensor product of judgments.

Let us use the above formalism to describe other combinations of judgments.

The tensor product of
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
is

\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash U\otimes V\;\mathsf{sort}

where
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x:U)
,
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y:V)
, and

U\otimes V=RS\begin{pmatrix}\alpha_{1}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&\alpha_{1}\otimes\beta_{N}&\alpha_{1}\otimes y^{V}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\alpha_{M}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&\alpha_{M}\otimes\beta_{N}&\alpha_{M}\otimes y^{V}\\
x^{U}\otimes\beta_{1}&\cdots&x^{U}\otimes\beta_{N}&-\end{pmatrix}=\partial(U\{x\}\square V\{y\}).

This implies that
(U\otimes V)\{xy\}=U\{x\}\square V\{y\}
.

Similarly, it can be checked that the tensor product of
\textbf{X}\vdash u:U
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
is

\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\vdash u\square V\{y\}:\partial(U\{u\}\square V\{y\}),

and that of
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V
is

\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash U\{x\}\square v:\partial(U\{x\}\medblacksquare V\{v\}).

These formulas can be extended in a straightforward way to ones for describing
J\odot J^{\prime}
when
J
or
J^{\prime}
is an equality judgment.

We now state the main result of this section:

Theorem 7.3
.

Let
\mathbb{A}
,
\mathbb{B}
and
\mathbb{C}
be generalized algebraic theories. Then
(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})\otimes\mathbb{C}
and
\mathbb{A}\otimes(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{C})
, both viewed over the alphabet
\Sigma(\mathbb{A})\otimes\Sigma(\mathbb{B})\otimes\Sigma(\mathbb{C})
, have the same derivable judgments. As a consequence, we obtain an isomorphism
(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})\otimes\mathbb{C}\cong\mathbb{A}\otimes(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{C})
.

We will sketch a proof that if
J
,
J^{\prime}
and
J^{\prime\prime}
are derivable judgments in
\mathbb{A}
,
\mathbb{B}
and
\mathbb{C}
, respectively, then
(J\odot J^{\prime})\odot J^{\prime\prime}
is derivable in
\mathbb{A}\otimes(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{C})
. By considering axioms
J
,
J^{\prime}
and
J^{\prime\prime}
, we conclude that every axiom in
(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})\otimes\mathbb{C}
is derivable in
\mathbb{A}\otimes(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{C})
, hence that every judgment derivable in
(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})\otimes\mathbb{C}
is also derivable in
\mathbb{A}\otimes(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{C})
. It can be verified analogously that, conversely, if a judgment is derivable in
\mathbb{A}\otimes(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{C})
, then it is derivable in
(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})\otimes\mathbb{C}
.

Let us fix some terminology: in what follows, by
derivable
or
provably equal
we mean so in
\mathbb{A}\otimes(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{C})
whenever we are dealing with judgments or contexts/expressions in
\Sigma(\mathbb{A})\otimes\Sigma(\mathbb{B})\otimes\Sigma(\mathbb{C})
. We also use the following convention: given derivable judgments
J
,
J^{\prime}
in the alphabet of
\mathbb{A}\otimes(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{C})
, we write
J\sim J^{\prime}
when
J
,
J^{\prime}
have the same interpretation in the contextual category
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{A}\otimes(\mathbb{B}\otimes\mathbb{C}))
. Precisely, one of the following holds:

–

J
,
J^{\prime}
are sort judgments that correspond to the same display map in
\mathcal{C}
.

–

J
,
J^{\prime}
are term judgments that correspond to the same section of a display map in
\mathcal{C}
.

–

J
,
J^{\prime}
are sort equality judgments, say
\textbf{X}\vdash U\equiv U^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\equiv V^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
, respectively, such that
(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\sim(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort})
and
(\textbf{X}\vdash U^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})\sim(\textbf{Y}\vdash V^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})
.

–

J
,
J^{\prime}
are term equality judgments, say
\textbf{X}\vdash u\equiv u^{\prime}:U
and
\textbf{Y}\vdash v\equiv v^{\prime}:V
, respectively, such that
(\textbf{X}\vdash u:U)\sim(\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V)
and
(\textbf{X}\vdash u^{\prime}:U)\sim(\textbf{Y}\vdash v^{\prime}:V)
.

In other words,
J=(\textbf{X}\vdash\blacksquare
) is derivable and
J^{\prime}
can be obtained from
J
by a combination of the following kinds of operations: replacing the context
X
by a provably equal one, and replacing a sort/term expression in
\blacksquare
by a provably equal one.

We check by induction on
h\geq 0
that the following three statements hold:

(A1)

If
\textsf{ht}(J)\textsf{ht}(J^{\prime})\textsf{ht}(J^{\prime\prime})=h
and
(J\odot J^{\prime})\odot J^{\prime\prime}
is derivable, then so is
J\odot(J^{\prime}\odot J^{\prime\prime})
, and
(J\odot J^{\prime})\odot J^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow J\odot(J^{\prime}\odot J^{\prime\prime})
.

(A2)

Given contexts
X
,
Y
,
Z
in
\mathbb{A}
,
\mathbb{B}
,
\mathbb{C}
, resp., such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Z})=h
, the judgment

(\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}\equiv\textbf{X}\otimes(\textbf{Y}\otimes\textbf{Z})\;\textsf{ctx}

is derivable.

(A3)

Consider derivable judgments
J
,
J^{\prime}
,
J^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathbb{A}
,
\mathbb{B}
,
\mathbb{C}
, respectively, each of which is a sort judgment or a term judgment, and such that
\textsf{ht}(J)\textsf{ht}(J^{\prime})\textsf{ht}(J^{\prime\prime})=h
. Let
K
be the context of the judgment
(J\odot J^{\prime})\odot J^{\prime\prime}
. Then, in the notation of
2.1
, the following terms are derivable and provably equal to each other in
K
:

\displaystyle(J\otimes_{t}J^{\prime})\otimes_{t}J^{\prime\prime},

\displaystyle\qquad J\otimes_{t}(J^{\prime}\otimes_{t}J^{\prime\prime}),

\displaystyle(J\otimes_{t}J^{\prime})\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime\prime},

\displaystyle\qquad J\otimes_{t}(J^{\prime}\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime\prime}),

\displaystyle(J\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime})\otimes_{t}J^{\prime\prime},

\displaystyle\qquad J\medblackcircle_{t}(J^{\prime}\otimes_{t}J^{\prime\prime}),

\displaystyle(J\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime})\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime\prime},

\displaystyle\qquad J\medblackcircle_{t}(J^{\prime}\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime\prime}).

Observe that, as we are working in
(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})\otimes\mathbb{C}
, by using term equalities
\varphi\otimes\psi\equiv\varphi\medblackcircle\psi
obtained by tensoring two term judgments we can derive equalities between the four expressions in the left column:

(J\otimes_{t}J^{\prime})\otimes_{t}J^{\prime\prime}\equiv(J\otimes_{t}J^{\prime})\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime\prime}\equiv(J\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime})\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime\prime}\equiv(J\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime})\otimes_{t}J^{\prime\prime}.

Before proceeding, let us make the statement more explicit. If
J=(\textbf{X}\vdash u:U)
,
J^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V)
and
J^{\prime\prime}=(\textbf{Z}\vdash w:W)
, then the above term expressions are

\displaystyle(u^{U}\otimes v^{V})\otimes w^{W},

\displaystyle\qquad u^{U}\otimes(v^{V}\otimes w^{W}),

\displaystyle(u^{U}\otimes v^{V})\medblackcircle w^{W},

\displaystyle\qquad u^{U}\otimes(v^{V}\medblackcircle w^{W}),

\displaystyle(u^{U}\medblackcircle v^{V})\otimes w^{W},

\displaystyle\qquad u^{U}\medblackcircle(v^{V}\otimes w^{W}),

\displaystyle(u^{U}\medblackcircle v^{V})\medblackcircle w^{W},

\displaystyle\qquad u^{U}\medblackcircle(v^{V}\medblackcircle w^{W}),

and we are claiming their derivability and equality in context
(\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}
. When some among
J
,
J^{\prime}
,
J^{\prime\prime}
are, instead, sort judgments, we suitably expand the context and modify the above expressions by using fresh variables in place of
u
,
v
or
w
. For example, if we replace
J
by
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
, then we consider

\displaystyle(x^{U}\otimes v^{V})\otimes w^{W},

\displaystyle\qquad x^{U}\otimes(v^{V}\otimes w^{W}),

\displaystyle(x^{U}\otimes v^{V})\medblackcircle w^{W},

\displaystyle\qquad x^{U}\otimes(v^{V}\medblackcircle w^{W}),

\displaystyle(x^{U}\medblackcircle v^{V})\otimes w^{W},

\displaystyle\qquad x^{U}\medblackcircle(v^{V}\otimes w^{W}),

\displaystyle(x^{U}\medblackcircle v^{V})\medblackcircle w^{W},

\displaystyle\qquad x^{U}\medblackcircle(v^{V}\medblackcircle w^{W})

in context
(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}
, where
x
is the chosen variable not occurring in
X
, and
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x:U)
.

Now, let us sketch how to prove statements (A1)-(A3) for a given
h\geq 0
assuming that they hold for
h^{\prime}<h
.

7.2
Sketch of proofs of (A1) and (A2)

Before studying (A1), let us prove a preliminary lemma: given contexts
X
,
Y
,
Z
in
\mathbb{A}
,
\mathbb{B}
,
\mathbb{C}
, resp. such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Z})\leq h
, the judgment
\partial((\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z})\equiv\partial(\textbf{X}\otimes(\textbf{Y}\otimes\textbf{Z}))\;\textsf{ctx}
is derivable.

We start by observing that (the object of
\mathcal{C}((\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})\otimes\mathbb{C})
corresponding to)
\partial((\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z})
is, in a canonical way, a limit of the diagram of projections

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\textbf{X}\otimes\partial\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\partial\textbf{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\partial\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\textbf{X}\otimes\partial\textbf{Y})\otimes\partial\textbf{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\partial\textbf{Y})\otimes\partial\textbf{Z},}}

i.e. where each arrow is dual to the corresponding inclusion between sets of variables – for example,
(\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}\rightarrow(\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\partial\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}
is given by lexicographically reindexing the family
(x_{i}y_{j}z_{k})_{(i,j,k)\in\{1,...,m-1\}\times\{1,...,n-1\}\times\{1,...,p\}}
whose entries are taken from the set of variables of
(\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}
.

Since
\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{X})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})
,
\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{Y})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})
and
\textsf{ht}(\partial\textbf{Z})<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Z})
, it follows from the induction hypothesis that each context in the above diagram is provably equal to the one having the same three factors but associated differently – i.e.
(\partial\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}\equiv\partial\textbf{X}\otimes(\textbf{Y}\otimes\textbf{Z})\;\textsf{ctx}
, etc. are derivable. Hence
\partial(\textbf{X}\otimes(\textbf{Y}\otimes\textbf{Z}))
is also, canonically, a limit of the diagram. This implies that the sequence of variables
\partial(((x_{1},...,x_{m})\otimes(y_{1},...,y_{n}))\otimes(z_{1},...,z_{p}))
is a morphism from
\partial((\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z})
to
\partial(\textbf{X}\otimes(\textbf{Y}\otimes\textbf{Z}))
, thus that
\partial((\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z})\equiv\partial(\textbf{X}\otimes(\textbf{Y}\otimes\textbf{Z}))\;\textsf{ctx}
is derivable.

7.2.1
Statement (A1)

Now, let us sketch a proof of (A1). We have different cases depending on the kinds of judgments being considered. Below, whenever applicable we let
\textbf{X}^{\prime}=(\textbf{X},x:U)
,
\textbf{Y}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Y},y:V)
and
\textbf{Z}^{\prime}=(\textbf{Z},z:W)
where
x
,
y
,
z
are the chosen variables not occurring in
X
,
Y
,
Z
, respectively.

•

J
is
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
,
J^{\prime}
is
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
, and
J^{\prime\prime}
is
\textbf{Z}\vdash W\;\mathsf{sort}
. Then

\displaystyle(J\odot J^{\prime})\odot J^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash U\otimes V\;\mathsf{sort})\odot(\textbf{Z}\vdash W\;\mathsf{sort})

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\partial((\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime})\vdash(U\otimes V)\otimes W\;\mathsf{sort}

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\partial((\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime})\vdash\partial((U\otimes V)\{xy\}\square W\{z\})\;\mathsf{sort}

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\partial((\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime})\vdash\partial((U\{x\}\square V\{y\})\square W\{z\})\;\mathsf{sort}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\partial((\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime})\vdash\partial(U\{x\}\square(V\{y\}\square W\{z\}))\;\mathsf{sort}

(calculation below)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes(\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime}))\vdash\partial(U\{x\}\square(V\{y\}\square W\{z\}))\;\mathsf{sort}

(lemma)

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes(\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime}))\vdash U\otimes(V\otimes W)\;\mathsf{sort}

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle J\odot(J^{\prime}\odot J^{\prime\prime}).

It remains to verify that
(U\{x\}\square V\{y\})\square W\{z\}\equiv U\{x\}\square(V\{x\}\square W\{z\})
is entrywise derivable in context
\textbf{K}=\partial((\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime})
. Writing

U=R(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{M}),\quad V=S(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{N}),\quad W=T(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{P}),

we must derive

RST(((\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{M},x^{U})\otimes(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{N},y^{V}))\otimes(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{P},z^{W}))

\overset{\textbf{K}}{\equiv}RST((\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{M},x^{U})\otimes((\beta_{1},...,\beta_{N},y^{V})\otimes(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{P},z^{W}))).

This corresponds to deriving in
K
the following equalities:

(1)

(\alpha_{i}\otimes\beta_{j})\otimes\gamma_{k}\equiv\alpha_{i}\otimes(\beta_{j}\otimes\gamma_{k})
.

Its derivability follows from (A3), via the induction hypothesis, since

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash\alpha_{i}:\mathcal{S}(\alpha_{i}))

\displaystyle<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}),

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash\beta_{j}:\mathcal{S}(\beta_{j}))

\displaystyle<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}),

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Z}\vdash\gamma_{k}:\mathcal{S}(\gamma_{k}))

\displaystyle<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Z}\vdash W\;\mathsf{sort}).

(Note that this is stronger than required to use the induction hypothesis: it is sufficient that one of these three inequalities be strict. This remark will be used repeatedly below.)

(2)

(\alpha_{i}\otimes\beta_{j})\otimes z^{W}\equiv\alpha_{i}\otimes(\beta_{j}\otimes z^{W})
.

(3)

(\alpha_{i}\otimes y^{V})\otimes\gamma_{k}\equiv\alpha_{i}\otimes(y^{V}\otimes\gamma_{k})
.

(4)

(\alpha_{i}\otimes y^{V})\otimes z^{W}\equiv\alpha_{i}\otimes(y^{V}\otimes z^{W})
.

(5)

(x^{U}\otimes\beta_{j})\otimes\gamma_{k}\equiv x^{U}\otimes(\beta_{j}\otimes\gamma_{k})
.

(6)

(x^{U}\otimes\beta_{j})\otimes z^{W}\equiv x^{U}\otimes(\beta_{j}\otimes z^{W})
.

(7)

(x^{U}\otimes y^{V})\otimes\gamma_{k}\equiv x^{U}\otimes(y^{V}\otimes\gamma_{k})
.

These cases are verified similarly to (1). Here, when applicable, we use
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
,
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
,
\textbf{Z}\vdash W\;\mathsf{sort}
, respectively, instead of
\textbf{X}\vdash\alpha_{i}:\mathcal{S}(\alpha_{i})
,
\textbf{Y}\vdash\beta_{j}:\mathcal{S}(\beta_{j})
,
\textbf{Z}\vdash\gamma_{k}:\mathcal{S}(\gamma_{k})
.

(8)

(x^{U}\otimes y^{V})\otimes z^{W}\equiv x^{U}\otimes(y^{V}\otimes z^{W})
.

The left- and right-hand sides equal
xyz
.

•

J
is
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
,
J^{\prime}
is
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
, and
J^{\prime\prime}
is
\textbf{Z}\vdash w:W
.

\displaystyle(J\odot J^{\prime})\odot J^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash U\otimes V\;\mathsf{sort})\odot(\textbf{Z}\vdash w:W)

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}\vdash(U\otimes V)\{xy\}\square w:\partial((U\otimes V)\{xy\}\medblacksquare W\{w\})

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}\vdash(U\{x\}\square V\{y\})\square w:\partial((U\{x\}\square V\{y\})\medblacksquare W\{w\})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}\vdash U\{x\}\square(V\{y\}\square w):\partial(U\{x\}\medblacksquare(V\{y\}\medblacksquare W\{w\}))

(calculation below)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes(\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Z})\vdash U\{x\}\square(V\{y\}\square w):\partial(U\{x\}\medblacksquare(V\{y\}\medblacksquare W\{w\}))

(A2)

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\odot(\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Z}\vdash V\{y\}\square w:\partial(V\{y\}\medblacksquare W\{w\}))

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle J\odot(J^{\prime}\odot J^{\prime\prime}).

Let us now check that

(U\{x\}\square V\{y\})\square w\equiv U\{x\}\square(V\{y\}\square w),

\partial((U\{x\}\square V\{y\})\medblacksquare W\{w\})\equiv\partial(U\{x\}\medblacksquare(V\{y\}\medblacksquare W\{w\}))

are entrywise derivable in
\textbf{K}=\partial((\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z})
.

In (
*
), if
w
is a variable, then the left- and right-hand sides are both
xyw
. Otherwise, write

U=R(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{M}),\quad V=S(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{N}),\quad w=t(c_{1},...,c_{p}).

Then (
*
) is

RSt(((\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{M},x^{U})\otimes(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{N},y^{V}))\otimes(c_{1},...,c_{p}))

\overset{\textbf{K}}{\equiv}RSt((\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{M},x^{U})\otimes((\beta_{1},...,\beta_{N},y^{V})\otimes(c_{1},...,c_{p}))),

so it suffices to derive, in context
K
, all equalities of one of the forms

(1)

(\alpha_{i}\otimes\beta_{j})\otimes c_{k}\equiv\alpha_{i}\otimes(\beta_{j}\otimes c_{k})
.

(2)

(\alpha_{i}\otimes y^{V})\otimes c_{k}\equiv\alpha_{i}\otimes(y^{V}\otimes c_{k})

(3)

(x^{U}\otimes\beta_{j})\otimes c_{k}\equiv x^{U}\otimes(\beta_{j}\otimes c_{k})
.

(4)

(x^{U}\otimes y^{V})\otimes c_{k}\equiv x^{U}\otimes(y^{V}\otimes c_{k})
.

These follow from (A3) since

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash\alpha_{i}:\mathcal{S}(\alpha_{i}))

\displaystyle<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}),

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash\beta_{j}:\mathcal{S}(\beta_{j}))

\displaystyle<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}),

\displaystyle\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Z}\vdash c_{k}:\mathcal{S}(c_{k}))

\displaystyle<\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Z}\vdash w:W).

For (
**
), write

U=R(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{M}),\quad V=S(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{N}),\quad W=T(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{P}).

Then the desired equality is

RST(\partial(((\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{M},x^{U})\otimes(\beta_{1},...,\beta_{N},y^{V}))\medblackcircle(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{P},w^{W})))

\overset{\textbf{K}}{\equiv}RST(\partial((\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{M},x^{U})\medblackcircle((\beta_{1},...,\beta_{N},y^{V})\medblackcircle(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{P},w^{W})))),

for which we must derive:

(1)

(\alpha_{i}\otimes\beta_{j})\otimes\gamma_{k}\equiv\alpha_{i}\otimes(\beta_{j}\otimes\gamma_{k})
.

(2)

(\alpha_{i}\otimes\beta_{j})\medblackcircle w^{W}\equiv\alpha_{i}\otimes(\beta_{j}\medblackcircle w^{W})
.

(3)

(\alpha_{i}\otimes y^{V})\otimes\gamma_{k}\equiv\alpha_{i}\otimes(y^{V}\otimes\gamma_{k})
.

(4)

(\alpha_{i}\otimes y^{V})\medblackcircle w^{W}\equiv\alpha_{i}\medblackcircle(y^{V}\medblackcircle w^{W})
.

(5)

(x^{U}\otimes\beta_{j})\otimes\gamma_{k}\equiv x^{U}\otimes(\beta_{j}\otimes\gamma_{k})
.

(6)

(x^{U}\otimes\beta_{j})\medblackcircle w^{W}\equiv x^{U}\otimes(\beta_{j}\medblackcircle w^{W})
.

(7)

(x^{U}\otimes y^{V})\otimes\gamma_{k}\equiv x^{U}\otimes(y^{V}\otimes\gamma_{k})
.

All of these follow from (A3).

•

J
is
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
,
J^{\prime}
is
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
, and
J^{\prime\prime}
is
\textbf{Z}\vdash W\equiv W^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
. Then

\displaystyle(J\odot J^{\prime})\odot J^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash U\otimes V\;\mathsf{sort})\odot(\textbf{Z}\vdash W\equiv W^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\partial((\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime})\vdash(U\otimes V)\otimes W\equiv(U\otimes V)\otimes W^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\partial((\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime})\vdash\partial((U\otimes V)\{xy\}\square W\{z\})\equiv\partial((U\otimes V)\{xy\}\square W^{\prime}\{z\})\;\mathsf{sort}

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\partial((\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime})\vdash\partial((U\{x\}\square V\{y\})\square W\{z\})\equiv\partial((U\{x\}\square V\{y\})\square W^{\prime}\{z\})\;\mathsf{sort}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\partial((\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime})\vdash\partial(U\{x\}\square(V\{y\}\square W\{z\}))\equiv\partial(U\{x\}\square(V\{y\}\square W^{\prime}\{z\}))\;\mathsf{sort}

(see below)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes(\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime}))\vdash\partial(U\{x\}\square(V\{y\}\square W\{z\}))\equiv\partial(U\{x\}\square(V\{y\}\square W^{\prime}\{z\}))\;\mathsf{sort}

(lemma)

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes(\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime}))\vdash U\otimes(V\otimes W)\equiv U\otimes(V\otimes W^{\prime})\;\mathsf{sort}

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle J\odot(J^{\prime}\odot J^{\prime\prime}).

For the remaining step, calculations analogous to those in the previous case show that each of the type equalities

\displaystyle\partial((U\{x\}\square V\{y\})\square W\{z\})

\displaystyle\equiv\partial(U\{x\}\square(V\{y\}\square W\{z\}))

\displaystyle\partial((U\{x\}\square V\{y\})\square W^{\prime}\{z\})

\displaystyle\equiv\partial(U\{x\}\square(V\{y\}\square W^{\prime}\{z\}))

is entrywise derivable in context
\partial((\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime})
.

•

J
is
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
,
J^{\prime}
is
\textbf{Y}\vdash V\;\mathsf{sort}
, and
J^{\prime\prime}
is
\textbf{Z}\vdash w\equiv w^{\prime}:W
. Then

\displaystyle(J\odot J^{\prime})\odot J^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\partial(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\vdash U\otimes V\;\mathsf{sort})\odot(\textbf{Z}\vdash w\equiv w^{\prime}:W)

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}\vdash(U\otimes V)\{xy\}\square w\equiv(U\otimes V)\{xy\}\square w^{\prime}:\partial((U\otimes V)\{xy\}\medblacksquare W\{w\})

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}\vdash(U\{x\}\square V\{y\})\square w\equiv(U\{x\}\square V\{y\})\square w^{\prime}:\partial((U\{x\}\square V\{y\})\medblacksquare W\{w\})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}^{\prime})\otimes\textbf{Z}\vdash U\{x\}\square(V\{y\}\square w)\equiv U\{x\}\square(V\{y\}\square w^{\prime}):\partial(U\{x\}\medblacksquare(V\{y\}\medblacksquare W\{w\}))

(see below)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes(\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Z})\vdash U\{x\}\square(V\{y\}\square w)\equiv U\{x\}\square(V\{y\}\square w^{\prime}):\partial(U\{x\}\medblacksquare(V\{y\}\medblacksquare W\{w\}))

(A2)

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\odot(\textbf{Y}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Z}\vdash V\{y\}\square w\equiv V\{y\}\square w^{\prime}:\partial(V\{y\}\medblacksquare W\{w\}))

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle J\odot(J^{\prime}\odot J^{\prime\prime}).

•

J
is
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
,
J^{\prime}
is
\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V
, and
J^{\prime\prime}
is
\textbf{Z}\vdash w:W
. Then

\displaystyle(J\odot J^{\prime})\odot J^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash U\{x\}\square v:\partial(U\{x\}\medblacksquare V\{v\}))\odot(\textbf{Z}\vdash w:W)

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}\vdash(U\{x\}\medblacksquare V\{v\})\square w\equiv(U\{x\}\square v)\medblacksquare W\{w\}:\partial((U\{x\}\medblacksquare V\{v\})\mathrel{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to5.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.75pt\lower-2.75pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}W\{w\})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}\vdash U\{x\}\square(V\{v\}\square w)\equiv U\{x\}\square(v\medblacksquare W\{w\}):\partial(U\{x\}\medblacksquare(V\{v\}\mathrel{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to5.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.75pt\lower-2.75pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 3.46 M -3.46 3.46 L -3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 3.46 Z M 3.46 -3.46}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 0 M -3.46 0 L -3.46 3.46 L 3.46 3.46 L 3.46 0 Z M 3.46 3.46}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}W\{w\}))

(see below)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes(\textbf{Y}\otimes\textbf{Z})\vdash U\{x\}\square(V\{v\}\square w)\equiv U\{x\}\square(v\medblacksquare W\{w\}):\partial(U\{x\}\medblacksquare(V\{v\}\mathrel{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to5.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.75pt\lower-2.75pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 3.46 M -3.46 3.46 L -3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 3.46 Z M 3.46 -3.46}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 0 M -3.46 0 L -3.46 3.46 L 3.46 3.46 L 3.46 0 Z M 3.46 3.46}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}W\{w\}))

(A2)

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\odot(\textbf{Y}\otimes\textbf{Z}\vdash V\{v\}\square w\equiv v\medblacksquare W\{w\}:\partial(V\{v\}\medblacksquare W\{w\}))

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle J\odot(J^{\prime}\odot J^{\prime\prime}).

It remains to verify that the following equalities are entrywise derivable in context
(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}
:

\displaystyle(U\{x\}\medblacksquare V\{v\})\square w

\displaystyle\equiv U\{x\}\square(V\{v\}\square w),

\displaystyle(U\{x\}\square v)\medblacksquare W\{w\}

\displaystyle\equiv U\{x\}\square(v\medblacksquare W\{w\}),

\displaystyle\partial((U\{x\}\medblacksquare V\{v\})\mathrel{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to5.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.75pt\lower-2.75pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 3.46 M -3.46 3.46 L -3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 3.46 Z M 3.46 -3.46}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 0 M -3.46 0 L -3.46 3.46 L 3.46 3.46 L 3.46 0 Z M 3.46 3.46}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}W\{w\})

\displaystyle\equiv\partial(U\{x\}\medblacksquare(V\{v\}\mathrel{\hbox to5.5pt{\vbox to5.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.75pt\lower-2.75pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 3.46 M -3.46 3.46 L -3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 -3.46 L 3.46 3.46 Z M 3.46 -3.46}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -3.46 0 M -3.46 0 L -3.46 3.46 L 3.46 3.46 L 3.46 0 Z M 3.46 3.46}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}W\{w\})).

These can be expanded and derived using (A3), in a way analogous to our approach in the previous cases.

•

J
is
\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort}
,
J^{\prime}
is
\textbf{Y}\vdash v:V
, and
J^{\prime\prime}
is
\textbf{Z}\vdash W\equiv W^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort}
. Then

\displaystyle(J\odot J^{\prime})\odot J^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y}\vdash U\{x\}\square v:\partial(U\{x\}\medblacksquare V\{v\}))\otimes(\textbf{Z}\vdash W\equiv W^{\prime}\;\mathsf{sort})

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime}\vdash(U\{x\}\square v)\square W\{z\}\equiv(U\{x\}\square v)\square W^{\prime}\{z\}:\partial((U\{x\}\medblacksquare V\{v\})\medblacksquare W\{z\})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime}\vdash U\{x\}\square(v\square W\{z\})\equiv U\{x\}\square(v\square W^{\prime}\{z\}):\partial(U\{x\}\medblacksquare(V\{v\}\square W\{z\}))

(see below)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\textbf{X}^{\prime}\otimes(\textbf{Y}\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime})\vdash U\{x\}\square(v\square W\{z\})\equiv U\{x\}\square(v\square W^{\prime}\{z\}):\partial(U\{x\}\medblacksquare(V\{v\}\square W\{z\}))

(A2)

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle(\textbf{X}\vdash U\;\mathsf{sort})\otimes(\textbf{Y}\otimes\textbf{Z}^{\prime}\vdash v\square W\{z\}\equiv v\square W^{\prime}\{z\}:\partial(V\{v\}\square W\{z\}))

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle J\odot(J^{\prime}\odot J^{\prime\prime}).

As in the previous cases, we can conclude by expanding the equalities

\displaystyle(U\{x\}\square v)\square W\{z\}

\displaystyle\equiv U\{x\}\square(v\square W\{z\})

\displaystyle(U\{x\}\square v)\square W^{\prime}\{z\}

\displaystyle\equiv U\{x\}\square(v\square W^{\prime}\{z\})

\displaystyle\partial((U\{x\}\medblacksquare V\{v\})\medblacksquare W\{z\})

\displaystyle\equiv\partial(U\{x\}\medblacksquare(V\{v\}\square W\{z\}))

and deriving them entrywise from (A3).

•

(J\odot J^{\prime})\odot J^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow J\odot(J^{\prime}\odot J^{\prime\prime})
can be proved analogously in the remaining cases, namely, when
J
,
J^{\prime}
,
J^{\prime\prime}
are, respectively, judgments of the following forms:

*

sort, term, sort

*

term, sort, sort

*

sort, sort equality, sort

*

sort equality, sort, sort

*

sort, term equality, sort

*

term equality, sort, sort

*

term, sort, term

*

term, term, sort

*

sort, sort equality, term

*

sort equality, sort, term

*

sort equality, term, sort

*

term, sort, sort equality

*

term, sort equality, sort

7.2.2
Statement (A2)

Consider contexts

\textbf{X}=(x_{1}:X_{1},...,x_{m}:X_{m}),\qquad\textbf{Y}=(y_{1}:Y_{1},...,y_{n}:Y_{n}),\qquad\textbf{Z}=(z_{1}:Z_{1},...,z_{p}:Z_{p})

in
\mathbb{A}
,
\mathbb{B}
,
\mathbb{C}
, respectively, such that
\textsf{ht}(\textbf{X})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Y})\textsf{ht}(\textbf{Z})=h\geq 1
. We will verify that
(\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}\equiv\textbf{X}\otimes(\textbf{Y}\otimes\textbf{Z})\;\textsf{ctx}
is derivable under the assumption that the analogous statement always holds with
0
, …,
h-1
in place of
h
.

By the lemma proved at the beginning of the section, the judgment

\partial((\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z})\equiv\partial(\textbf{X}\otimes(\textbf{Y}\otimes\textbf{Z}))\;\textsf{ctx}

is derivable. Moreover, applying (A1) to
J=(\partial\textbf{X}\vdash X_{m}\;\mathsf{sort})
,
J^{\prime}=(\partial\textbf{Y}\vdash Y_{n}\;\mathsf{sort})
,
J^{\prime\prime}=(\partial\textbf{Z}\vdash Z_{p}\;\mathsf{sort})
yields derivability of

\partial((\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z})\vdash(X_{m}\otimes Y_{n})\otimes Z_{p}\equiv X_{m}\otimes(Y_{n}\otimes Z_{p})\;\mathsf{sort}.

By combining (
*
) and (
**
) we conclude that
\textbf{X}\otimes(\textbf{Y}\otimes\textbf{Z})
is a context and is provably equal to
(\textbf{X}\otimes\textbf{Y})\otimes\textbf{Z}
in
(\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B})\otimes\mathbb{C}
.

7.3
Sketch of proof of (A3)

Let
J
,
J^{\prime}
,
J^{\prime\prime}
and
K
be as in the statement of (A3), and consider the following list of equalities – to be verified – in context
K
:

(E1)

(J\otimes_{t}J^{\prime})\otimes_{t}J^{\prime\prime}\equiv J\otimes_{t}(J^{\prime}\otimes_{t}J^{\prime\prime})
.

(E2)

(J\otimes_{t}J^{\prime})\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime\prime}\equiv J\otimes_{t}(J^{\prime}\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime\prime})
.

(E3)

(J\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime})\otimes_{t}J^{\prime\prime}\equiv J\medblackcircle_{t}(J^{\prime}\otimes_{t}J^{\prime\prime})
.

(E4)

(J\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime})\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime\prime}\equiv J\medblackcircle_{t}(J^{\prime}\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime\prime})
.

Remark 7.4
.

Observe that it is possible to reduce the set of cases to be checked by repeatedly using term equalities
\varphi\otimes\psi\equiv\varphi\medblackcircle\psi
obtained by tensoring two term judgments. Indeed, this shows derivability of

(J\otimes_{t}J^{\prime})\otimes_{t}J^{\prime\prime}\equiv(J\otimes_{t}J^{\prime})\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime\prime}\equiv(J\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime})\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime\prime}\equiv(J\medblackcircle_{t}J^{\prime})\otimes_{t}J^{\prime\prime}

in
K
. This will be combined with the fact that if
J
is either a sort judgment or
\textbf{X}\vdash x:U
for a variable
x
, then, by definition,
\otimes_{t}
and
\medblackcircle_{t}
give identical expressions when one of the arguments is
J
; similarly for
J^{\prime}
,
J^{\prime\prime}
. For example, if
J
is of that form, then
